# Univer SDK Showcase

## Local validation artifacts

Generated reports and transient screenshots belong in the ignored `test-results/`
directory, not in version control. Historical delivery notes below and in individual
demo READMEs may reference local reports or diagnostic scripts that are not shipped.
Those references record past investigations, not runnable repository resources or
current acceptance guarantees. Use the retained `package.json` test commands for
supported checks; coverage evidence only lists maintained repository files.

## Willow and Driftwood native delivery

Both current ten-file standalone exports were independently compared byte for
byte with the sources. Reviewed native covers and dedicated package test entries
are connected to the catalog. No old fixture or inspection controls were restored.

Willow (`test-results/willow-responsive-native/report.json`) records 23/28 strict
gates and six literal recipes. Only host/reading width controls remain; native
footer zoom drives reflow. Full EN/ZH packs, five official stylesheets, original
fourteen paragraphs, native text history, selection during resize and interrupted
initialization disposal have selected evidence. Five failures remain: phone/150%
layout error, chart model/frame width mismatch, drawing-resource string ordering
after resize history, and two recovery cases losing explicit undefined transform
properties. The existing lower-level reflow mutation is not a Facade setter.

Driftwood (`test-results/driftwood-native/report.json`) records 38/40 strict gates
and four literal recipes. Native link popups, external and internal navigation,
editing/removal, both locales, theme preservation and same-ID recovery have
selected evidence. External requests were intercepted during validation. Native
enumeration still returns only the first rich-text span, and initial insertion
Undo removes the original B7 null cell record. Both remain strict failures; source
parity and a working popup do not erase these limitations.

## Cedar lazy-loading recovery delivery

The reviewed normal-export cover and current native test are connected to the
catalog and package entry. The selected report at
`test-results/cedar-lazy-native-r3-recovery/report.json` records 8/9 strict gates
and 28 checks. Actual deferred SDK/CSS requests, EN/ZH failed-import guidance,
explicit browser refresh recovery, cancellation, release, reconstruction and
eleven-file source parity have evidence. A failed module import does not present
an ineffective Retry button or automatically reload unsaved page work.

The native first-edit Undo still leaves `C4.t = 2`; this raw model difference
is retained as a failure. Initialization-error retry and recovery from internal
SDK disposal exceptions are not certified. JSON download is a snapshot, not
Office binary conversion. This is partial acceptance, not a completed capability.

## Mossbrook native canvas delivery

The interrupted agent's completed report was independently inspected against the
current eleven-file export; all source bytes match. The personally reviewed
normal-production cover is now connected to both catalog locales, and the package
entry runs `test-mossbrook-native-complete.mjs`. No fixture/readback/history panel
was restored; only actual extension style/layer registration controls remain.

`test-results/mossbrook-native-complete/report.json` records 32/36 strict gates,
six literal recipes and no runtime errors. Native percentage/formula repaint,
layer pixels and disposal handles, geometry/scroll/freeze/zoom, worksheet scope,
same-owner themes, initial EN/ZH, narrow viewports, restored fresh input and
pre-Steady/during-calculation disposal have selected evidence. Four raw failures
remain: initial input Undo changes type/style representation, Redo retains an
extra style, row-height Undo leaves row records, and new-owner recovery changes
the empty defined-name resource. They are not normalized or counted as accepted.


## Pelican native find/replace delivery

The package entry now uses `test-find-native-cleanup.mjs`, and the personally
reviewed settled native cover is connected to both catalog locales. The original
two small worksheets remain; there is no duplicated host finder, fixture picker,
Reset or readback panel. Both official CSS bundles and full EN/ZH packs ship with
the same exported factory. Eleven README literals execute as authored.

`test-results/pelican-find-native-deep/report.json` records 17/25 strict gates.
Actual native search/replacement, case/whole-cell changes, selection/workbook
scope, Unicode/zero/literal punctuation, real formula replacement and complete
formula Undo/Redo pass. Ten-file production source/CSS parity passes separately.

Eight failures remain distinctly classified: four text Undo model differences,
same-ID empty-resource serialization, one delayed SDK callback after owner
disposal, a whitespace-only safety expectation and an isolated non-string input
probe outside the TypeScript contract. The last two are not presented as ordinary
native UI defects. Every text Redo is exact; those passes do not erase Undo or
lifecycle failures. Matching highlights are independent of editing selections,
and changing native whole-cell options invalidates results until Find is run.


## Willow responsive cleanup checkpoint

The responsive-width factory no longer renders fixture/reset/reload/empty,
fixed selection/reveal/note insertion, history controls or raw geometry readback.
Original narrative, columns, supplies, native chart and map initialization remain.
Native Grid UI is enabled; five official stylesheets and complete EN/ZH core,
drawing, chart, column and table packs are shared with source export. Preview

TypeScript, scoped lint and shared source checks pass for this cleanup. This is
not runtime acceptance: host/native zoom integration, actual reflow and chart
frame behavior, narrow/high-zoom errors, complete history/recovery and pending
asynchronous initialization cleanup are under independent verification. Existing
SDK-operation reflow is not described as an invented FDocument setter. No data or

## Custom-event native delivery

The package entry now uses `test-aster-custom-event-native.mjs`. The personally

`test-results/aster-custom-event-native/report.json` records 30/34 strict gates
and four literal recipes. Native A1/B2 context handling, C cancellation, B–D
overlap, allowed F deletion, actual before/after model timing, independent
consumer disposal, guard re-subscription, full same-owner recovery, fresh restored

## Native custom menu and shortcuts delivery

The current package entries use `test-custom-menu-native.mjs` and
`test-swift-shortcuts-native.mjs`. Both personally inspected settled native covers
are connected to the EN/ZH catalog. Original minimal data is unchanged. Neither
case reintroduces fixture/reset/selection buttons or a raw model inspector.

Swift passes 14/19 strict gates and 13/13 literals in
`test-results/swift-shortcuts-native/report.json`. Real C3 row clearing, dragged
range clearing, empty no-op, host-input/cell-editor focus exclusions, registration
release/reinstall, fresh reconstructed shortcuts and simulated macOS Backspace
have evidence. The three complete Undo failures add cell type fields; their Redo
models match. Two same-ID recovery failures change defined-name serialization.
The one compact hint and Host input demonstrate actual shortcut behavior.

Custom Menu passes 5/13 strict gates with 36 checks and all six TS plus two JS
literals in `test-results/custom-menu-native-r3/report.json`. Actual ribbon items,
the two-level context submenu, classic overflow, rejection guards and old-owner
cleanup run against the native SDK. Dynamic disabled affordances still fail;
rejecting an invalid callback is not equivalent to a disabled menu item. Raw
Undo and same-ID resource recovery differences are retained, including fresh
editing and normal-export history. All measured Redo models match. The normal
export gate is therefore not labeled fully passing merely because paint/CSS do.

Both exports include official Core CSS and complete EN/ZH packs. Themes preserve
the current owner. These scoped results do not certify all native editor actions,
React/Next integration or resolution of the recorded SDK differences.

## Content scope: feature clarity versus composed showcases

Single-feature demos should use the smallest clear dataset that demonstrates the
existing capability and its meaningful variations. Do not add unrelated business
stories, extra documents or volume solely to make a feature demo look richer.
Overall product showcases and Embed compositions should retain rich, coherent
business content appropriate to the workflow and referenced examples. Distinct
capabilities matter in both categories; cosmetic changes alone do not justify
another demo. Neither category relaxes real Facade behavior, matching source and
output, official exported CSS, complete locales or native interaction checks.

## Research, document tables and incident Canvas delivery

Three inspected native covers are now connected to the bilingual catalog and
their package test entries point at the current native acceptance scripts.
These are partial capability results, not three fully accepted products:

| Case | Strict gates | Literal examples | Current evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Research paper | 29/33 | 14/14 | `test-results/research-paper-native-acceptance/report.json` |
| Cedar document tables | 35/46 | 25/25 executed | `test-results/cedar-tables-native/report.json` |
| Incident response Canvas | 8/9 | 9 TS + 1 JS | `test-results/incident-response-native-r2/report.json` |

Research retains the original two-page paper and Appendix A; the guarded Appendix B
recipe creates page three. Four failures retain typing/fresh-owner Undo optional
arrays, margin Undo section identity and heading styling paragraph identity.
Cedar retains four tables, two-column prose, a CC0 image and three native charts.
Full same-ID edited/baseline recovery passes after real word-count readiness;
the earlier transient transform mismatch is not a confirmed serialization defect.
Ten recipe-history gates (including two explicitly multi-mutation recipes) and
the isolated auto-hyphenation column regression remain failures. Incident retains
free-endpoint connectors and the original response/risk story, with actual move,
resize and same-ID recovery evidence. Multiline text replacement retains the old
first line, and a single Undo does not revert the entire editing session; this
does not assume that a multiline session is one history transaction.

All three independent deliveries pass nine-file source/CSS parity. Main-agent
inspection confirmed actual content in the selected published captures. These
checks do not certify all menus, all devices, Next integration or Office conversion.
The older incident "in progress" section below is a historical checkpoint.

## Native company knowledge space

page navigation now lives in the same factory as the exported editor; Reset,
Mark reviewed and activity controls are removed. Grid, complete EN/ZH core
resources, official CSS and same-owner theme handling are included.

`test-results/knowledge-space-native-acceptance/report.json` is strict **32/37
gates**, with **14/14 literal recipes** passing. Three-page navigation, native
paint/input, full-space same-ID reconstruction, empty/restore, theme, locale and
disposal have selected evidence. Five failures retain native/fresh/review Undo
adding optional arrays, paragraph styling changing a paragraph ID, and swallowed
slash input. No resource normalization or SDK patch is used.

The published cover is the inspected `normal-production-settled.png`, not the
earlier blank generic capture. Final nine-file source/CSS parity is recorded in
`test-results/knowledge-space-native-export-parity/report.json`. The shared
regression's knowledge branch now edits the actual review paragraph and checks
navigation persistence and absence of removed buttons; it passed against the
same standalone production build. That run does not certify the Next wrapper or
the other, independently selected regression branches. Archived review policy
is not SDK read-only enforcement; related-page prose is not link resolution.

- Relational Table creation / Lumen: 20 snippets; 29/30 selected gates pass, including actual
  title/Person editing with exact full Undo/Redo, all table recipes, whole-owner
  reconstruction, checkpoint, four datasets, five full EN/ZH packs and themes.
  Strict native Person-name painting still fails. Current report:
  `test-results/lumen-native-final/report.json`; nine-file/four-CSS export:
  `test-results/lumen-native-export-ui/report.json`.
- Canvas lifecycle / Tern: nine snippets plus two reconstruction recipes; seven
  of nine selected gates pass, including real pointer/ArrowRight history, exact
  owner recovery and seven complete EN/ZH/CSS packs. Sticky replacement/editor
  errors and heading Undo remain strict failures. Reports:
  `test-results/tern-native/report.json` and `tern-native-export-ui/report.json`.
- Slides sizes / Rivet: eight original pages and 20 snippets compare dimensions,
  inheritance, scaling and rotated/edge overflow natively. Deep-ocean, cream,
  mint and coral pages use 24 transparent native text shapes. Sixteen of seventeen
  selected gates pass, including complete native text and size/drawing history,
  five full EN/ZH/CSS packs and themes; only rebuilt main-canvas attachment fails.
  Current reports: `test-results/rivet-size-native-visual/report.json` and
  `rivet-size-export-visual/report.json`.
- PDF markup / Aster: 12 snippets and 17 selected gates pass, including actual
  text-selection drag, Properties and native annotation pixels; five complete
  EN/ZH/CSS packs and same-owner themes. Save/reopen, full native property/history
  coverage and binary Print/Exchange remain unaccepted. Current runtime:
  `test-results/pdf-markup-native-final/report.json`.
  Strict independent startup passes seven additional gates in
  `test-results/pdf-markup-export-startup/report.json`: real main-page words and
  annotation pixels, native Properties, no skeleton, and initial Chinese labels
  without a post-load language switch. Ten-file source/CSS parity passes in
  `test-results/pdf-markup-native-export-ui/report.json`; the old false-positive
  overlay capture is retained in `pdf-markup-export-skeleton-history`.

The four reviewed native screenshots are now explicitly passed from metadata
through the catalog to the existing Showcase cards. Other cases keep their icon
fallback until a reviewed image is registered. `node scripts/test-showcase-catalog-images.mjs`
checks all four real PNGs, EN/ZH title/image propagation and the unchanged fallback;
it does not claim a full Next catalog-browser acceptance run.

Counts are unchanged: 165 registered routes and 270 blueprints, with 0 verified,
127 partial and 143 unassessed. All four routes remain partial. Older control-panel
reports below are historical, not acceptance of the current source. The export
UI verifier now explicitly waits for the native startup skeleton to disappear;

## Formula scope expansion / data-driven product compositions

The Formula catalog now contains **30 implemented routes, all partial**, up from three:
Sheet/Relational Table sources drive Modern Docs, Traditional Docs, Slides, Canvases and native
formula-backed charts. Both Sheet@Slide and Slide@Sheet have independent Float/Tab
plans; embedding direction is distinct from data dependency. Multi-source and
multi-output business scenarios are included. See the
[Formula Demo Catalog](capabilities/formula-demo-catalog.md).

Current registry/plan counts are **149 pages and 270 capability blueprints**.
Historical Formula expansion checkpoint (superseded by the current counts above):
the coverage ledger was **109 partial, 0 fully verified, 161 unassessed**;
new plans are not completed demos. Harbor's six explicit-ID formula examples have
runtime evidence, but native source keyboard input changes the host, inactive
Float rendering is blank, and native source Print fails. Its new runtime evidence
has not yet been added to the capability ledger. Beacon's new four-page native
Formula Shape demo has eight successful initial results and working selected
source recalculations, but Relational Table editing disrupts the Slides workbench; its strict
runtime report fails and it is not yet ledgered. Earlier snapshots below are historical.

Nova / `embed/sheet-to-slides-tab` adds a real Sheet data page in the native
Slides page list, distinct from Lumen's Float. Ten literal examples drive thirteen
Formula Shapes across three original navy, coral and plum operating-review pages.
Current canvas values, native Grid editing/value history, exact Redo, correct-source
Print menu, active-source disposal, EN/ZH themes and independent source/CSS export
have selected evidence. Strict runtime remains FAIL on off-page source writes and
extra style metadata after first Undo.

Violet / `embed/base-to-slides-tab` adds eight original editorial records and
thirteen native formulas on three slides. Twelve literal examples distinguish
ready-piece counts from word-weighted coverage; display-name changes, filtered
views, hidden source edits, null/zero, errors/recovery, current canvas results,
off-page writes, native title/number editing with exact Undo/Redo and disposal
pass selected production tests. Eleven-file source/CSS export passes. Formula
at that checkpoint had 14 implemented routes out of 30 planned, with 12 ledgered partial; none
are fully accepted.

Orchid / `embed/base-to-slides-float` adds a separate Relational Table Float pipeline story:
six opportunities, twelve native formulas and three forest/paper/plum slides.
After native source activation, all twelve literal snippets update all three
current canvases and preserve authored layouts. Separate native amount typing,
activated-Float disposal and eleven-file official-CSS export pass selected checks.
`test-results/embed-orchid-formula-activated-next/report.json` passes EN/ZH literal
examples and theme/model checks. The strict independent
`test-results/embed-orchid-formula-activated/report.json` still FAILS on off-page
writes, native title Undo and fullscreen. Passive writes also retain historical
failures. This is not a completed demo or a global SDK focus fix.

Cinder / `embed/base-to-modern-doc` adds an original incident-handoff story:
eight Relational Table records drive ten inline formulas without rewriting the modern prose.
Seventeen literal examples, native current-canvas results, filtering/hidden edits,
empty-queue recovery, native typing, same-fullscreen exact Undo/Redo, disposal and
eleven-file official-CSS export pass selected checks. The strict native report
remains FAIL on error classification and Undo after fullscreen reentry. Explicit
native source mapping fixes the earlier rename failure and adds idempotent binding,
mapping removal and live repair. EN/ZH integration passes all seventeen examples
and full-model theme checks. See its README and `test-results/embed-cinder-formula-binding-recovery/report.json`.

The shared pre-preview explanation card was removed at the user's request.
Demo metadata and exported README examples remain; the unused rendering component
was deleted. `test-results/embed-nova-no-guide-card/report.json` confirms EN/ZH
card absence, ten executable snippets and preserved theme ownership/models.
The 77 affected test scripts now assert card absence rather than obsolete guide
counts; card screenshots and success labels were updated without removing native
SDK interaction checks. Static parsing covers 210 scripts, with no stale guide
count/heading-parent references found; changed scripts pass lint/diff checks.
See `test-results/showcase-guide-migration/report.json`. This is not a claim that
all 77 complete runtime suites passed: those suites retain their independent
native-behavior requirements and known failures.

The earlier `test-results/showcase-card-free-final/report.json` passes EN/ZH selected-page
checks at 1440/390/320px: all sixteen old card headings are absent, an injected
heading is correctly rejected, README/source navigation remains usable, and the
Violet Relational Table/Slides models and API owner survive layout changes without horizontal
page overflow. This is outer-page layout evidence, not mobile editor acceptance:
at 320px the native Slides thumbnail pane consumes almost the entire preview,
so the editable slide was not usefully visible in that build. Earlier failures from capturing a replaced cold-start iframe and
assuming a CodeMirror class instead of the actual read-only Sandpack viewer are
retained separately. No SDK or runtime behavior was patched for these tests.

Violet's subsequent responsive build uses FUniver.setUIVisible to expose the
native page list only when requested below 640px, then fits the same slide canvas.
`test-results/embed-violet-responsive-native/report.json` passes initial narrow
paint, Enter/Space navigation across all three pages at 320/390px, desktop return,
and all twelve existing formula examples, native editing/history and disposal.
`test-results/violet-responsive-export-ui/report.json` verifies the rebuilt
eleven-file export and native white CSS. The outer EN/ZH card-free checks finish
at all widths, preserving data/content and owner (native saved view zoom may
change), but `test-results/showcase-card-free-responsive/report.json` remains FAIL
because Next's development RSC client throws Performance.measure negative-time
errors. Full narrow-screen Grid/Relational Table editing and delivery remain unaccepted.

### Current Harbor native Embed acceptance

`embed/slides-in-sheets-float` is the first registered case of the new 41-case
native Embed expansion. A twelve-line community-ferry budget accompanies an
original two-slide navy/mint decision brief. The selected production report
`embed-slide-float-production-raw` verifies actual child painting, native
activation/page navigation, preserved rich-text edits, host recalculation with
unchanged child data, and error-free selected teardown. `embed-slide-float-export-parity`
verifies nine-file source parity and official SDK CSS. The beta.2 portal cleanup
workaround and remaining lifecycle/performance gaps are documented
in the case README and `capabilities/sdk-issues.md`. This remains partial coverage.

Collaborative version/revision history is outside this frontend-only collection. Four unimplemented history blueprints have been moved to `capabilities/excluded-collaborative-history.json` for traceability and are no longer counted: Sheets, Modern Docs, Slides and Canvases. Refer to the current ledger above; pages and capabilities are not interchangeable completion units. Ordinary native Undo/Redo and explicitly labelled local snapshot serialization are distinct from collaborative revision history. Reference local/advanced SDK demos when deciding which plugins and operations belong in this collection.

The current interaction policy is visual-first: authored pages demonstrate basic formatting, while users operate the native Ribbon. The user explicitly removed the exception for generic fixture controls: no fixture selector, Load, Reset or Inspect toolbar should cover the editor. Collapsing or hiding duplicate buttons is insufficient; remove their UI and handlers. Native Exchange and Print plugins should provide client file/print workflows. Audit each demo before claiming this policy is complete.

Color direction now follows the user-provided Deep Ocean CI reference with varied page/case palettes, not a single navy skin. See `capabilities/visual-direction.md`. Lumen is the first native-only migration: its host toolbar and decorative host styles are deleted; the deck opens with Deep Ocean, cyan/lilac, mint and coral/gold pages. Text uses transparent native shapes instead of white boxes. The official editor CSS remains untouched. Exchange Client, Slides Exchange Client and Slides Print are registered, and the standalone export includes all seven directly imported official stylesheets. Other demos have not yet been migrated to the no-fixture-panel policy.

### Historical migration evidence

The following Lumen reports predate the final removal of its fixture toolbar and the Deep Ocean restyling. They remain useful regression records, not acceptance of the current native-only source.

Six older Slides demos (`theme-and-background`, `page-size-and-overflow`, `layouts-and-placeholders`, `save-restore-deck`, `slide-lifecycle`, `reorder-and-sections`) previously selected Grid while hiding header and toolbar. They now expose the native Grid and use a case-specific 1040px preview. `test-results/slides-six-grid-canvas/report.json` passes official white/flex styling, one populated native Grid with 17 command controls per case, and main canvas heights of 453–518px. Screenshots were reviewed. Other demos keep their existing preview heights. `test-showcase-ribbon.mjs` now rejects hidden Slides ribbons; the screenshot tools use a stable preview attribute rather than a height class.

`slides-theme-grid-regression` passes the full theme/background regression before host-button reduction. `slides-lifecycle-grid-regression` retains three known beta.2 failures: title Undo preserves new rich text, its painted title does not revert, and insertion Undo selects the wrong prior page. Menu visibility passes are not full capability acceptance. The native-first host-control audit is still ongoing; Theme and Background is the first case removing duplicate Undo/Redo host buttons in favor of real Ribbon commands.

`slides-theme-native-history-final/report.json` now passes with actual native Ribbon Undo/Redo and no duplicate host history buttons. Host actions focus their owning presentation before mutation, so the native history status tracks the correct unit. The initial native-history run exposed the disabled native Undo state; a separate development chunk-load failure is retained in `slides-theme-native-history-focused`. The final runner also checks disabled native history boundaries and the reduced 15-control host keyboard sequence. This is ordinary local Undo/Redo, not a collaborative history feature. Theme/background and formatting host controls still require further reduction under the updated native-first policy.

Theme selection now uses the native Themes gallery too: the duplicate target selector, Apply theme and invalid-theme buttons are removed. `slides-theme-native-picker-export-settled/report.json` passes all twelve native theme selections, four background types, eight authored pages, native Undo/Redo and narrow-screen checks, with exact parity for all eight exported files. The independently built export includes 114.46 kB of official/host CSS. The runner rejects duplicate host theme/history controls. Its gallery screenshot waits for finite animations and asserts the official white background and opacity 1 throughout the ancestor chain; the earlier `slides-theme-native-picker-localhost/native-themes-gallery.png` caught the entrance fade and is not final visual evidence.

Documentation-site acceptance remains incomplete. `slides-theme-native-picker` retained an obsolete 12-action assertion after the guide changed to 11. `slides-theme-native-picker-current` failed because the test's 127.0.0.1 origin was rejected by the development server; using its existing localhost origin avoids that error without weakening origin policy. `slides-theme-native-picker-localhost/report.json` completes theme/background interactions and both EN/ZH 4-variant/11-action/4-state guides, but fails on two React pre-mount state-update warnings. A subsequent focused playground probe records no warning (`mount-warning-stacks.json`); that does not explain or clear the full-run failure. Background/navigation host controls and formatting demos still require native-first reduction. No catalog-wide runtime-completion claim is made.

### Lumen background and navigation reduction

The current Lumen host has six controls (one fixture selector and five buttons), down from twelve. Background/color inputs, Apply background, Use inherited background, the Slide selector and Show slide are removed. The Background study fixture authors solid, gradient, diagonal-pattern and original-image fills on its first four pages, plus the fixed Pilot background. Users navigate native thumbnails and edit/reset through Format Background. The installed panel cannot select a pattern; its authored snapshot rendering is explicitly distinguished from editable native fill types. The API list no longer advertises removed host calls. Grid configuration follows `univer-pro/examples/src/slides-advanced/main.ts`; feature-specific registrations and the five official CSS imports remain shared by preview/export.

`slides-theme-native-background-export-collapsed/report.json` passes exact eight-file export parity, twelve native themes, eight authored slides, four native background Reset/Undo/Redo pixel round trips, a native Gradient fill edit and its Redo pixels, local snapshot reload, six-control keyboard navigation and 760/390/320px layouts. The independently rebuilt export is recorded in `lumen-background-export/exports.json`: 14,682.65 kB JS (3,500.76 kB gzip) and 114.46 kB CSS (17.09 kB gzip); the large-bundle warning remains. The reviewed pattern/image screenshots show the real white native background panel, not substitute host UI.

`slides-theme-native-background-next-collapsed/report.json` separately passes the same interactions plus light/dark recreation and EN/ZH guides with live iframes (4 variants, 11 explained host/native actions, 4 states). Both final reports contain zero browser errors. This does not diagnose or erase the earlier intermittent React warning. Retained `*-export` / `*-next` failures exposed an obsolete assumption: native thumbnails directly activate pages and add no Undo entry, unlike the removed host Facade navigation. `*-navigation` used the panel's internal operation rather than its actual Ribbon command ID; `*-panel` incorrectly nested a root-scoped radio locator; `*-label` assumed closing unmounts the panel. The final test clicks the visible native radio label, checks its checked state, and proves panel closure by `aria-expanded=false`, zero allocated width and recovered canvas width. It never forces a click or injects a background mutation.

Remaining work includes native-control reduction in the other demos, lifecycle fault-path acceptance, broader native accessibility and performance. These scoped results do not mark the whole collection or the Slides capability blueprint verified.

## SDK events to a host activity panel

`embed/univer-events-to-host` adds eight original Juniper launch milestones, real value-change payloads, native selection callbacks, explicit unsubscribe/resubscribe and full in-memory saved snapshots. Four variants, seven actions and four states have EN/ZH explanations. Host-only Clear and Save are labelled as such; Save calls `FWorkbook.save()` but is not advertised as a nonexistent SDK save-completed event. Native Grid and official CSS are shared by Preview and the independent export.

`test-results/host-events-standalone-final/report.json` passes exact parity for all ten exported files; actual host writes; native selection/edit/Undo; unsubscribing both activity listeners while SDK state continues updating; immutable saved content across later edits; invalid integer-input preservation; latest-12 activity retention; exact full-snapshot Reset; Empty/Boundary fixtures; and 760/390/320px host keyboard checks. `host-events-next-guides/report.json` separately passes those interactions and working EN/ZH 4/7/4 guide pages. Both record zero browser errors and zero demo write requests. The public thumbnail is the reviewed standalone `default.png`.

The retained `host-events-standalone-scoped`, `host-events-standalone-steady` and `host-events-next` reports did not receive generic `Event.SelectionChanged` after programmatic `activate()`. The first workbook-callback probe also expected a callback from activation and failed. The final test instead proves activation by `getSelection().getActiveRange()` and tests `FWorkbook.onSelectionChange()` with actual native selections. This distinction is documented in the example; it is not evidence that the generic event works or a conclusive diagnosis of a generic-event SDK defect. `host-events-native-selection` subsequently failed because the test expected a bounded 12-entry feed to grow beyond its cap; final checks assert the newest actual payload instead.

The retained `host-events-ownership-faults-first/report.json` exposes a real initialization leak: readback registration failed after SDK construction but outside the cleanup boundary. Registration now shares the workbook initialization failure boundary and tracks each returned lifetime handle. `host-events-ownership-registration-fixed/report.json` passes 14 actual owners and 54 tracked handles with no observed late callbacks: three sequential theme/unmount/remount cycles, fixture subscription replacement, explicit unsubscribe/resubscribe, injected readback/lifecycle/workbook/selection registration failures, an exception after actual listener disposal and parent unmount before Steady. Exceptions remain observable, rather than swallowed to make the test pass. This does not prove cleanup after SDK construction throws without returning an owner or a registration allocates resources without returning its handle.

After that fix, `host-events-registration-standalone/report.json` passes the rebuilt ten-file export and all interaction checks; `host-events-registration-next/report.json` passes the selected Next playground and EN/ZH guides with no browser errors. The later README-only update was re-exported and checked byte-for-byte across all ten displayed files; runtime sources remained unchanged.

The independent export is `test-results/host-events-export/exports.json`; only this case was built with Vite 8.2.2 and SDK 1.0.0-beta.2. It contains `index-p4kEmv_F.js` (6,250,901 bytes) and 102.19 kB / 15.33 kB gzip official/host CSS. The bundle-size warning remains. ## Multi-instance isolation

`scripts/test-univer-instance-isolation.mjs` checks the `standalone-embed-003` requirement before introducing a public implementation. Run it with `SHOWCASE_VITE_MODULE` pointing to an installed Vite module (an independent export can supply it) and optional `SHOWCASE_RESULTS_DIR`. It serves only a two-owner Sheets harness on port 4183 and closes it after the run. Distinct workbook IDs are the default; `SHOWCASE_DUPLICATE_UNIT_IDS=1` is a strict negative control, not a compatibility mode.

`test-results/instance-isolation-full/report.json` verifies real edits, opposite-workbook snapshot preservation, exact reset, selective disposal and surviving native editing/Undo. It deliberately remains failed because a dark South instance turns the light North workbench dark through the SDK's document-level `univer-dark` class. See `capabilities/sdk-issues.md`; merely rendering two editors does not satisfy the planned isolation requirement. `SHOWCASE_ISOLATION_FRAMES=1` instead creates actual iframe documents; `instance-isolation-frames/report.json` passes both contrasting themes and the same isolation operations without changing official CSS. This feasibility test alone is not public-case acceptance.

`embed/multiple-isolated-instances` is now the public iframe integration: six original North workshop items and six different South garden items, independent Default/Empty/Boundary fixtures, explicit host-invalid-input Error, four documented variants, seven actions and four states in EN/ZH. Each child owns its installed SDK, official CSS, native Grid and Facade operations. North remains light; South remains dark. This is trusted same-origin framing, not a security sandbox or cross-origin command bridge.

`isolated-regions-standalone-cleanup/report.json` passes exact parity for ten exported files and all actual regional controls: independent price writes and formula totals, numeric `getRawValue()` readback, full JSON download, exact single-region Reset, invalid-input preservation, real selective release, surviving native editing/Undo, fixture isolation and 760px stacking. `isolated-regions-next-cleanup/report.json` separately passes the selected Next playground with nested iframe documents. No browser errors or demo write requests were recorded. The initial selector failure and formatted-price failure remain under `isolated-regions-standalone` and `isolated-regions-standalone-scoped`; host button updates now target only `.region-controls button`, and numeric fields no longer receive formatted currency strings. The first Next run retained obsolete Strict Mode controls; disposal now removes the old host immediately, aborts pending init/write waits and finishes actual owner cleanup.

The previous cleanup reports proved DOM behavior but did not prove parent-unmount owner disposal. The retained `isolated-regions-parent-ownership-fixed` report fails that stronger check. Current layout-effect teardown reaches child windows before React removes them, while cancellable passive-effect mounting avoids discarded Strict Mode owners. `isolated-regions-commit-ownership-expanded/report.json` now passes ordinary, pending write, pending Release and pending Reset parent unmounts: all eight observed core owners dispose with no console errors. Earlier failed layout-only experiments remain recorded; a disposal callback alone does not certify every internal resource.

`isolated-regions-commit-standalone/report.json` passes the rebuilt ten-file export, full regional interactions, official white/dark Grid styling and 760/390/320px host keyboard checks. `isolated-regions-commit-next/report.json` separately passes the same interactions and both EN/ZH guide pages. Both report no browser errors or demo write requests.

The independent project is listed in `test-results/isolated-regions-export/exports.json`. It builds only this case with Vite 8.2.2 and SDK 1.0.0-beta.2. Its current JS is 6,251.52 kB / 1,587.94 kB gzip, with 102.33 kB / 15.41 kB gzip combined official/host CSS and lazy language chunks. The large-bundle warning remains. Strict independent/project TypeScript and targeted lint/format pass; 95-case source/CSS/ribbon checks are static policy checks, not all-feature acceptance. Coverage is 0 verified / 57 partial / 149 unassessed. Full accessibility, additional resource fault instrumentation and frozen time remain outstanding in the ledger.

The Showcase is the public, runnable demo catalog for Univer SDK. It covers Sheets, Modern Docs, Traditional Docs, Slides, Canvases, Relational Tables, PDFs, and Embed.

## Deferred editor delivery

### Bounded owner cleanup

`lazy-editor-faults-baseline/report.json` reproduces a host cleanup that never settles when the actual SDK calculation wait is wrapped with a deliberately pending promise. This is fault injection, not a claim that the installed SDK ignores its timeout. The deferred editor now applies independent ten-second deadlines to initial/write/teardown waits, interrupts initial/write waiting on owner disposal, attempts stopCalculation after a failed teardown wait, and releases both host event handles and the core owner even when another cleanup throws. Creation failure follows the same all-resources cleanup and preserves the original cause. Host counters explicitly count initialized editors and successful cleanups, not live owners after a reported cleanup error.

`lazy-editor-faults-final/report.json` passes six injected boundaries with sixteen disposed owners, detached old canvases, zero remaining tracked host events at each fault boundary, successful reloads and no browser errors. These include held/rejected calculation, throwing listener cleanup, combined workbook creation/listener failure, missing Rendered and parent removal during Rendered. The first `lazy-editor-faults-fixed` attempt exposed a harness ordering error: deferred Sheets Facade registration overwrote an earlier createWorkbook wrapper. Instrumentation now follows module registration; this harness intentionally does not prove network deferral. `lazy-editor-faults-current` retains a run interrupted by a source-edit hot reload; the final run uses stable source throughout.

`lazy-editor-bounded-standalone/report.json` repeats all four real production interaction groups without errors or demo writes. `lazy-editor-bounded-export/report.json` verifies ten exact export files, white/theme/flex styles and native canvas paint. The refreshed deferred module is 6,244.64 kB / 1,584.36 kB gzip; official CSS stays 101.14 kB / 15.03 kB gzip. Strict standalone/project TypeScript, targeted lint/formatting and the 94-case static source/CSS/ribbon gates pass. Full capability acceptance remains partial: core-dispose exceptions, pre-import/write parent removal, real network/module failures, external formulas, global time and broader accessibility still need coverage.

`embed/lazy-load-editor` adds an SDK-free overview with two summary cards, a below-the-fold route-budget editor, four fixtures, eight documented actions and four states in EN/ZH. It references the official Sheets lazy-registration and Embed Studio lazy-iframe examples, while demonstrating a whole-editor dynamic import. Twelve original delivery routes use varied volumes, distances and rates. Host loading/cancellation is explicitly distinguished from real Facade cell writes, selection, formula readback and workbook JSON export. The editor uses official preset CSS and Grid; its section fills the host viewport (minimum 640px), so the documentation preview does not crop a hard-coded oversized editor.

Selected independent build: `test-results/lazy-editor-export/exports.json` identifies the ten-file export. Vite emits separate host CSS and a 101.14 kB / 15.03 kB gzip official editor stylesheet, plus a deferred 6.24 MB / 1.58 MB gzip SDK module and language chunks. The large-chunk warning remains; deferred delivery is not payload optimization. Strict standalone and showcase TypeScript pass. `lazy-editor-export-parity-responsive/report.json` verifies exact source files, white native background, SDK theme/flex styles and real canvas paint.

`lazy-editor-standalone-final/report.json` passes actual production import deferral/cancellation, native editing and Undo/Redo, formula totals, full JSON downloads, invalid-write preservation, release/reload, four fixtures, exact Reset and narrow keyboard controls. `lazy-editor-details/report.json` also passes both EN/ZH guides with 4/8/4 entries and working iframe editing. `lazy-editor-ownership-current/report.json` verifies seven real owners disposed through three theme/unmount cycles, without browser errors. Initial ownership attempts exposed test assumptions that all previews mount immediately and have only one host during asynchronous cleanup; the harness now activates the new idle host before retaining the same owner-count/disposal assertions. The earlier export-parity height failure is retained, not presented as a CSS failure.

The catalog has 94 registered cases; capability assessments remain 0 verified / 56 partial / 150 unassessed. This case still needs frozen SDK time, broader loader/disposal faults, cross-browser accessibility and payload work as recorded in `capabilities/coverage.json`. A passing selected case or static catalog gate is not full-plan completion.

After replacing the fixed editor height with a host-relative height, `lazy-editor-standalone-responsive/report.json` repeats all four production interaction groups successfully, and `lazy-editor-export-parity-responsive/report.json` passes at a 939px native standalone height. The final documentation capture in `lazy-editor-capture-responsive/showcase-screenshots-VuRpaB/report.json` passes at a 579px native height within the 640px preview, with official white background, flex layout and opaque ancestors. The inspected image shows the host controls, native Grid and sheet tabs without the prior crop and is promoted to `public/assets/showcase/embed-lazy-load-editor.png`. The earlier oversized capture is retained only as intermediate evidence.

## Development CSS hot replacement

The intermittent transparent/unstyled editor is now reproduced with a real stylesheet-removal trace, not inferred from a later passing screenshot. `test-results/embed-css-cold-trace/report.json` records a successful CSS download followed by webpack HMR removing the only SDK/host stylesheet link. The installed Next 16.3.4 CSS extraction callback delegates to `_N_E_STYLE_LOAD`, whose App Router registry deduplicates the unchanged href and resolves immediately. HMR then removes the old link while the newly created replacement tag remains detached.

`next.config.mts` restores mini-css-extract-plugin's standard DOM insertion and actual load/error handling **only for development client compilation**. The existing plugin, extracted CSS bytes, imports, native theme/ribbon rules, server configuration and production path remain in place. ## Embed lifecycle failure boundaries

`embed/mount-dispose-remount` now bounds every Rendered/calculation wait to 10 seconds and cancels the Rendered wait when its enclosing host is removed. Teardown still waits for the calculation session; if that wait fails it requests installed `FFormula.stopCalculation()` and continues attempting workbook unload, each listener release and core owner disposal. Original and cleanup errors are aggregated, not converted to successful actions. Remount/Reset stop on a reported cleanup failure; a released owner can subsequently be mounted explicitly. There is no host DOM clearing that pretends to release the SDK.

`test-results/embed-lifecycle-faults-baseline/report.json` reproduces the old unbounded Rendered wait. `embed-lifecycle-faults-complete/report.json` passes twenty-owner teardown/recovery checks against the actual SDK and Preview: withheld Rendered notification, rejected calculation wait, a calculation-wait promise that never settles, post-unload exception, listener-disposal exception, workbook-creation exception and enclosing React removal during a held Rendered wait. Existing Apply/Remount/Mount pending teardown and dark/light/dark replacement also pass, without browser errors. These faults are explicitly injected by the test at Facade boundaries; they are not invented user-facing states, a replacement SDK, or proof that arbitrary external formula engines acknowledge cancellation. The source exporter contains none of the instrumentation.

The new nine-file independent export at `<USERPROFILE>/AppData/Local/Temp/univer-embed-safe-gPOhqu` is listed in `test-results/embed-lifecycle-safe-export/exports.json`. It installs 138 packages offline, passes strict standalone ES2022 TypeScript and builds only this case with Vite 8.2.2 (1,748 modules). Entry `index-DWBFdvtU.js` is 6,255.24 kB / 1,589.16 kB gzip. Official CSS remains `index-B_z17OX0.css`, 103.26 kB / 15.51 kB gzip, plus lazy language chunks. The large-entry warning remains. `embed-lifecycle-safe-export-ui/report.json` verifies all nine exact source files, native white/theme/flex styles and real text paint.

The failure-boundary implementation also passes `embed-lifecycle-safe-cold/report.json` (served theme patch, hydrated EN/ZH sidebar and live iframe), `embed-lifecycle-safe-details/report.json` (all seven localized/interaction groups), and `embed-lifecycle-safe-standalone/report.json` (all six independent production interaction groups). These reports contain no browser errors or demo write requests; documentation Server Actions are recorded separately. The 93-case style-isolation report has no failures. These are selected-case acceptance results, not full catalog completion.

Synchronous main-thread blocking, exceptions inside core disposal itself, more complex external asynchronous calculation cancellation, globally frozen SDK time, native accessibility/browser behavior and the independent initial-load timeout remain open. The historical initial lifecycle checkpoint below predates these failure-boundary improvements. No additional blueprint is counted complete by this update.

## CRM host-command inputs and full snapshots

`embed/crm-quote-calculator` now implements the missing host product, integer quantity, monthly USD price, discount, currency and illustrative FX inputs in blueprint `standalone-embed-004`. It extends the existing Northstar Robotics quote, not a second demo. Five EN/ZH variants, nine explained actions and four repeatable fixtures distinguish host drafts, native edits, preset writes, currency conversion, selection and complete snapshot operations. Amounts come only from SDK formulas: `getDisplayValue()` makes the host's visible totals match native formatting. All prices remain USD; FX is explicitly fictional and editable. Product selection does not silently change price; Enterprise is the explicit pricing preset.

Apply validates every host field before writing A4:D4/F4:G4 with `setValues()` and formatting E4:E11 with `setNumberFormat()`. These are separate SDK commands, not an atomic undo group. Unchanged Apply/Read/Preset buttons disable themselves. Native edits change totals but do not erase an unapplied form draft; Read sheet into form explicitly replaces that draft without any workbook writes. Select calculated total activates E11. JSON download and Save & reload use full `FWorkbook.save()` output, not a sampled cell range or XLSX conversion.

The expanded `scripts/test-crm-quote.mjs` checks real native B4 edits, SDK errors, three complete default resets with stable workbook IDs, six invalid host inputs preserving every snapshot field, EUR/JPY/USD and custom FX, actual native currency/percentage paint, keyboard E11 selection, exact downloaded/edited-reloaded snapshots and two loads of each default/empty/boundary/error fixture. Unknown fixture IDs are rejected before disposal. Native 390px editing and host controls at 760/390/320px remain tested. Documentation also checks theme replacement and both localized 5/9/4 guides with live iframe actions. Document-shell Server Action requests are separately recorded; demo mutations to external services are not allowed.

## Ribbon defaults and layout comparison

Desktop SDK initialization explicitly uses `ribbonType: 'grid'` in both Preview and exported code. `sheets/slim-via-plugin` and `docs/slim-via-plugin` deliberately retain `classic` for their smaller plugin sets. Preset-based starters use Grid. Mobile/Node cases do not receive desktop ribbon settings. Existing intentional toolbar/header visibility choices in focused or embedded cases are unchanged; choosing a ribbon layout does not force a hidden toolbar open.

`pnpm test:showcase:ribbon` audits all 93 registered source exports: 90 desktop initializations, 88 default Grid and two classic exceptions. This is a source-policy check, not proof that all 93 demos have passed runtime acceptance. `pnpm test:showcase:ribbon-ui sheets/list-validation docs-traditional/page-setup sheets/slim-via-plugin` visits only these explicit cases; use `SHOWCASE_ORIGIN` for the running scoped server. The selected native Grid toolbars measure 88px; the slim classic toolbar measures 24px. White theme, official flex CSS, opacity and populated native controls are checked without injected CSS.

`sheets/custom-menu` offers Grid (default) and Single-row (classic), with four EN/ZH variants and seven described action groups. Use the native `⋮` overflow for single-row actions that do not fit. The installed beta.2 Facade does **not** expose `setRibbonType`, although newer reference source does. The example instead recreates the SDK owner using real `ribbonType` preset configuration and `FWorkbook.save()`: workbook content/formatting and selection survive, while Undo history is cleared. Reset keeps the selected layout; a new visit or theme replacement starts in Grid. SDK reload changes the empty `SHEET_DEFINED_NAME_PLUGIN` representation from `''` to `'{}'`; tests allow only that exact empty-resource normalization and compare the rest of the complete snapshot. This is not a claim of byte-identical raw resource serialization.

Run `pnpm test:showcase:custom-menu` against the scoped documentation URL, or set `SHOWCASE_DEMO_URL` to an independent build. Full native action tests cover Grid/classic transitions, overflow actions, actual canvas fill, approval, Undo/Redo, reset, narrow layouts and EN/ZH iframe interactions. Historical screenshots and standalone exports from before this policy are not evidence of the new layouts; regenerate only the selected case before promoting a new thumbnail. Broader per-case visual regression, native screen-reader/touch behavior, interrupted layout replacement and the known grouped-ribbon-submenu defect remain open.

## Capturing native UI without masking CSS defects

Start only the required cases with `pnpm dev:showcase sheets/list-validation`, then run `pnpm screenshot sheets/list-validation` in a second terminal. Pass the same explicit slug list to both commands. `SHOWCASE_ORIGIN` defaults to `http://localhost:3030`; it can point to an already running selected-demo server. The screenshot command never starts a server or builds the full catalog. Missing/unknown slugs fail before browser launch.

Each run writes a new `showcase-screenshots-*` directory under `test-results` (or `SHOWCASE_RESULTS_DIR`) with a JSON report and only successful PNGs. Review them before promoting any image to `public/assets/showcase`. The capture uses a fresh light-mode context and the actual preview container without injected CSS, hidden controls, background replacement, or error-page fallback. It waits for exposed readiness, a full-size native canvas, fonts and finite animations, then checks the native white workbench, SDK theme/layout styles and ancestor opacity. Console/runtime errors fail the capture. Native translucent overlay canvases and deliberately colored document content are not forced white.

`pnpm test:showcase:screenshot` exercises this contract using synthetic browser fixtures; it is not SDK visual acceptance. It covers invalid selections, white/transparent backgrounds, missing theme/layout CSS, ancestor fading/opacity, delayed or missing full-size canvases, incomplete mount, missing editor, and browser failures. The gate intentionally rejects non-editor/terminal-only examples rather than silently producing substitute images. Feature-specific interactions, popup appearance, canvas content and dark-mode correctness still require the respective demo tests and visual review.

On 2026-09-05, the final scoped live `sheets/list-validation` capture passed with native `rgb(255, 255, 255)`, `--univer-gray-0: #FFFFFF`, flex layout and all ancestor opacities equal to 1, with no browser errors (`test-results/showcase-screenshots-g9a3CO/report.json`). Its screenshot was visually inspected. The development module audit contained only this demo and zero document MDX modules. All twelve synthetic browser cases and two invalid CLI selections passed (`test-results/screenshot-contract-9ovPtX/report.json` records the browser cases). Selected lint/formatting, the 83-case CSS export check and scope regression also passed. The capture-contract command is included in CI, but has only been run locally so far. This verifies that selected capture, not every catalog thumbnail or all feature behaviors.

## Traditional Docs typography report

Current responsive acceptance: `test-results/doc-fonts-responsive-standalone-final/report.json` and `doc-fonts-responsive-details-final/report.json` pass. The latter includes both localized guides and live iframe actions. Tests now cover duplicate native markers, eighteen alternate-family applications across all six script specimens, and native fit/100% zoom at 760/390/320 px. They measure the actual white paper pixels (approximately 667/331/267 px in standalone), not just a CSS width or configured zoom. View changes preserve body text/styles and page geometry; narrow native styling, reload and Reset retain content. Automatic resize preserves an existing SDK error. Same-viewport full snapshot assertions remain intact; cross-viewport content checks do not pretend runtime drawing coordinates must stay fixed.

Run `pnpm dev:showcase docs-traditional/fonts-fallback-and-glyphs` and `pnpm test:showcase:doc-fonts`. `SHOWCASE_DETAILS=1` includes English/Chinese guides and real embedded actions. The Alder Archive report starts with twelve native pages, six distinct multilingual samples, an acquisition table and an original baseline reference figure. Four variants explain font stacks, size/weight, target scope and scripts; eight action groups (including the view selector) and four states describe their individual outcomes. View calls the installed SetDocZoomRatioOperation via the real syncExecuteCommand Facade; it does not scale the editor with CSS or rewrite font sizes. Native toolbar zoom remains usable until the next width resize or mount reapplies the selected host view.

## Sheets list validation: native options and input enforcement

Run `pnpm dev:showcase sheets/list-validation` and `pnpm test:showcase:list-validation`. Seven variants compare single/multiple selection, fixed/live H2:H6 sources, chip/arrow/plain rendering, blank policy, invalid-input enforcement and boundary values. Rule construction, application/removal, sample writes, target selection, validation readback and snapshot reload use public Facades. Native multi-select adds/removes individual options; its stored value is SDK JSON serialization, not necessarily comma-separated text. The guide distinguishes native STOP rejection from WARNING acceptance and from programmatic writes. Sample buttons explicitly name Paper/Unknown because editing source cells can change their validity; identical writes and clearing already-empty data are disabled. The readback retains the SDK's column-major matrix and adds B2–B31 address labels.

### List validation follow-up: states, exact snapshots and native history

The current implementation adds **Load fixture** and **Download snapshot JSON** to the shared factory (eleven action buttons, nine documented action groups). The four executable states are mixed original intake, 30 blank categories, an invalid batch and original values without validation. Loading is explicit and replaces edits; merely changing the selector does not mutate the workbook. The invalid batch retains Glass, lowercase ceramic, Unknown, blank, numeric zero and the last-row multi-value so users can inspect actual failures. B34 is now read from the SDK instead of echoed from a constant. Reset restores the original date; reload preserves an edited date.

`list-validation-states-standalone/report.json` and the warmed `list-validation-states-warm/report.json` pass native single-dropdown Undo/Redo, all prior non-clipboard interactions, keyboard fixture loading and real JSON downloads. Full downloaded workbook equality is required after save/reload. Full reset equality ignores only the newly generated validation-rule UIDs; it preserves every other cell/style/geometry/resource field. The documentation run also checks seven variants, nine action groups, four described states, the four-level sidebar and real actions in both localized iframes. `list-validation-states-final` remains a failed report: first detail-route compilation raised a React script warning. A warm pass does not certify that cold path; the cause remains open in the ledger.

The fresh eight-file export is `<USERPROFILE>/AppData/Local/Temp/univer-validation-states-tPTs3l`, with 142 separately installed packages. Strict standalone/project TypeScript, lint, formatting, source parity and CSS-isolation gates pass. `list-validation-states-export-ui/report.json` confirms the exact exported files, official native white/theme/flex rules and a 697.3px workbench. Vite 8.2.2 builds 1,758 modules; `index-viUr5Es_.js` is 6,429.31 kB / 1,630.08 kB gzip, while `index-BwRIfr5z.css` stays 103.24 kB / 15.65 kB gzip. Large-bundle warnings remain.

**Clipboard is a separate strict failing gate:** `pnpm test:showcase:list-validation:clipboard` (or `node scripts/test-list-validation.mjs --clipboard`). Both `list-validation-crlf-sdk/report.json` and `list-validation-crlf-standalone/report.json` read back Windows clipboard input `Wood\r\nNot a material\r\nPaper`, then observe native values `Wood `, `Not a material ` and `Paper`. No input line contained a trailing space. The first category consequently becomes invalid. SDK `convertTextToTable()` splits only on `\n`, retaining carriage returns; the HTML conversion path turns them into spaces. The tests preserve exact equality and do not trim values. The earlier `clipboard-input` run incorrectly assumed LF-only clipboard transport and is superseded by the CRLF-aware input check, not by changing cell-value expectations. The runtime and EN/ZH guides disclose the defect. Immediate teardown after a rule change also remains an independent failing SDK gate; neither failure is waived by source/style or normal interaction passes.

The latest public `sheets-list-validation.png` is the inspected `list-validation-states-export-ui/editor-first.png`: the actual production editor after collapsing host controls and waiting for the native dropdown fade to finish. This is a user-reachable view, not an editor CSS override. `list-validation-states-ownership/report.json` again fails the immediate-unmount gate with every owner disposed but pending SDK row-height errors. Test URL defaults now match `dev:showcase` port 3030; explicit `SHOWCASE_DEMO_URL` selects another port.

## Sheets big data: million-row capacity with sparse bulk windows

Run `pnpm dev:showcase sheets/big-data` and `pnpm test:showcase:big-data`; `SHOWCASE_DEMO_URL` selects the documentation playground or production standalone. The old module eagerly created 1,000,000 × 10 string cells and then cloned the whole workbook. It is replaced by a one-million-row native worksheet with 100 initial records and deterministic 250/1,000/5,000 × 10 windows. Marlow reservoir rows include sample/site/batch text, decimal measures, zero flow, missing pH, negative variance, statuses and unassigned owners instead of repeated coordinate strings.

The [SpreadJS large-write guidance](https://developer.mescius.com/spreadjs/docs/BestPractices/SettingLargeAmountsofData) informed the rectangular bulk-write comparison, while installed Univer beta.2 Facades define the implementation. The demo calls `setRowCount`, one `setValues` per window, `setActiveRange`, `scrollToCell`, bounded `setValue`/`clearContent`, one-step Undo/Redo, `save` and fresh-ID recreation. Top/middle/bottom/custom positions are explicit host choices. The 30-jump value is labeled host API-call time, not FPS or renderer throughput; no scroll listener is presented as SDK incremental loading. JSON is a sparse workbook snapshot, not XLSX.

Beta.2 `setRowCount(10_000)` retains already populated cells at rows 500,001–1,000,000, and an active cell there becomes invalid for `getActiveCell`. The capacity action first moves the public selection to A1 and clears only tracked window tails through Facade before reducing capacity. Because that action has clear operations plus row-count change, one Undo restores capacity but does not restore all cleared windows; no atomic-host-action promise is made. The demo does not allocate ten million cells to manufacture a stress claim. See `capabilities/sdk-issues.md`.

`test-results/big-data-documentation-empty` passes the full documentation run: actual canvas text at rows 1, 500,000 and 995,000, viewport/active-cell readback, 10K capacity history, invalid input preservation, native keyboard edit, bounded sparse JSON, reload/Empty/Reset, themes, EN/ZH guide 5/5/5, hydrated navigation and narrow hosts. `test-results/big-data-built-final` repeats feature checks against the production Vite build. `test-results/big-data-ownership-final` passes three edited theme/unmount/remount cycles with seven disposed owners. `test-results/big-data-export-ui-final` verifies the exact nine exported files plus Core CSS, `rgb(255,255,255)` workbench and `--univer-gray-0: #FFFFFF`; white is not a host cover.

The independent export at `<USERPROFILE>/AppData/Local/Temp/univer-big-data-Yej0db` installed 138 packages with pnpm 12.1.0 and built 1,748 modules with Vite 8.2.2. Main assets are `index-D8HgvdO1.js` (6,254.53 kB / 1,589.10 kB gzip) and `index-pXc5kgld.css` (102.58 kB / 15.39 kB gzip), plus language chunks; the bundle warning remains. The public 1278×640 thumbnail is a real opaque native capture. Blueprint 032 remains partial for controlled performance/memory benchmarks, larger resident datasets, cross-browser/DPR/input-latency matrices, automatic/server/worker loading, structural content and deeper accessibility/failure paths. The ledger is 0 verified, 43 partial and 162 unassessed; the named owner-disposal audit now has no legacy Preview findings.

Three SDK limitations remain explicit. `node scripts/test-images-setters-sdk.mjs` is an independent renderer-free installed-package regression: direct source/crop/rotation setters retain their changed data after Undo. The strict `test-results/images-standalone-format-strict/report.json` fails that invariant and the SVG filename invariant below. `SHOWCASE_OBSERVE_KNOWN_DEFECTS=1` permits only the reproduced 46px/20px offset and the exact disclosed A4.png filename and reports it explicitly; observation passes are not full acceptance. It includes both themes, EN/ZH guide counts, hydrated tree and working iframe crop actions. Four Next server-action POSTs from the documentation shell are retained in the report; no editor/file uploads are permitted. PNG/JPEG source staging tests include repeat selection, empty/mismatched/corrupt/oversized/4097px input and unchanged prior data on rejection. Native cell download preserves the exact original SVG bytes but names the file A4.png; the button and status warn that no PNG conversion occurs and the original SVG should be renamed .svg. Other source formats are not certified by this assertion; JSON download is a SDK snapshot, not XLSX.

Run `pnpm dev:showcase sheets/csv-import-plugin`, then `pnpm test:showcase:csv-parser` and `pnpm test:showcase:csv`. The initial expanded-panel export failed the 600px minimum-height check; it was corrected by bounding host controls, not by weakening the native-style gate. The temporary project also passes strict TypeScript on `src/index.ts` and `src/vite-env.d.ts` using the workspace compiler with bundler resolution and DOM libraries.

Export preparation now includes installed DefinitelyTyped packages only for actual exported dependencies and adds the standard Vite client declaration for CSS imports. This fixes missing Papa Parse/CSS types without copying the documentation's toolchain. Source tests cover the generated manifest. Existing older temporary exports should be regenerated before current parity checks; their earlier reports are historical, not validation of this revised export shape. Runtime code and authored CSS are unchanged by this packaging step.

`csv-ownership-final/report.json` passes three theme/unmount/remount cycles after real imports and one held file-read teardown: eight owners disposed, old canvases detached, delayed old draft ignored, functional remount and no errors. Host frames/listeners, menu registration and SDK subscriptions are released. The static catalog now has 82 CSS imports/isolated stylesheets (44 custom / 38 mount-only); 15 other legacy Previews still fail owning-instance disposal. Full-catalog feature coverage remains unproven.

This custom CSV case does not complete the broader browser import/export blueprint: it does not supply the regional sales workbook, XLS/XLSX/Exchange round trips, automatic typing, worksheet resizing or CSV workbook export. Parsing is intentionally bounded (1 MiB, 2,000 rows, 100 columns, 10,000 cells) and destination dimensions are checked. Protected/merged targets, all CSV dialects, cross-browser/touch accessibility and physical file-dialog cancellation remain unverified. [SpreadJS Import & Export](https://developer.mescius.com/spreadjs/demos/features/spreadjs-file-format/overview/) remains the broader format/options reference; [Papa Parse documentation](https://www.papaparse.com/docs) supplies the parser's delimiter/quote/empty-row/error contract. These are references, not claims of feature parity.

## Crosshair highlighting: native bands, palette and real selection

Run `pnpm dev:showcase sheets/crosshair-highlighting`, then `pnpm test:showcase:crosshair`. The empty setup-only sample now shares one factory, original Oriole community rehearsal data, eight variants, eight actions and five states in English/Chinese. Two distinct weeks include zero, fractional and missing hours, Unicode names, a merged programme heading and native SUM formulas. Enable/Disable use installed Facade methods, disable already-satisfied actions and show actual state/events. Selection, sheet switch, bounded A1 navigation and snapshot reload use public Facade methods too. Empty/Reset recreate the owner, restoring native palette and enabled defaults; Reload preserves instance settings and edited cells, returns to C4 and clears history.

The test is strict by default: `test-results/crosshair-standalone-strict-mobile/report.json` fails on the beta.2 native palette auto-enable event omission (false → true but event count 10 → 10, not 11), with no browser errors. `SHOWCASE_OBSERVE_KNOWN_DEFECTS=1` explicitly observes that defect and continues; `crosshair-documentation-mobile/report.json` and `crosshair-standalone-mobile/report.json` pass with status `passed-with-known-sdk-defect`, not full SDK acceptance. They verify band pixels, excluded active cells, unchanged cell formatting, native drag/arrow selection, merged/edge/full-dimension variants, two-sheet state, native toolbar and palette, actual 30%/15% alpha changes, native editing and formula recalculation across reload, invalid-address preservation, empty/reset, keyboard controls, 760/390/320 widths and fresh mobile entry. Documentation additionally verifies both themes, both localized guides, hydrated tree navigation and working iframe buttons.

The SDK normalizes `4:4` to `A4:H4` and `C:C` to `C1:C24`; the test now checks actual canonical output. It also normalizes reversed rectangles, so the host validates each endpoint before rectangle activation instead of claiming to reject an already-normalized range. Public `scrollToCell` restores context after distant navigation and reveals the target column on narrow screens. Initial tests retained the wrong canonical-address expectation, and later palette tests sampled transitions before the popup closed; those diagnostic failures remain. The initial strict documentation run recorded an incremental Next ChunkLoadError; passing warmed runs do not resolve the shared cold-shell issue.

Preview and the eight-file export share Core/Crosshair SDK CSS plus scoped host styles. The independently installed project is `<USERPROFILE>/AppData/Local/Temp/univer-crosshair-LuqE8K`, recorded in `test-results/crosshair-export/exports.json` (139 installed packages). Vite 8.2.2 produces `index-Cz8dATrF.js` (6,262.84 kB / 1,591.53 kB gzip) and `index-CJey6Mxl.css` (103.84 kB / 15.50 kB gzip); large-chunk warnings remain. `crosshair-export-ui-mobile/report.json` passes exact exported bytes, native flex/theme rules and opaque-white workbench (`rgb(255, 255, 255)`, `--univer-gray-0: #FFFFFF`). Canvas overlay pixels legitimately carry alpha; they composite over that native white background. No editor CSS override or SDK patch is used to fake the native appearance.

`crosshair-ownership-mobile/report.json` passes three theme/unmount/remount cycles after real disable actions, seven owners all disposed and no errors. Static source/CSS/style-isolation checks cover 82 cases (43 custom / 39 mount-only stylesheets); the owner static gate still fails for 16 other legacy Previews. This case does not fulfill the separate workbook-selection blueprint, so the 200-blueprint ledger is unchanged. Programmatic color/opacity APIs are absent from the inspected installed Facade; native palette accessibility, multiple disjoint selections, frozen viewports, touch-only editing and cross-browser behavior remain unverified. ## Find and Replace: native matching and meaningful controls

The passing warm run does not fix that shell issue; the earlier incremental chunk-loading failure is retained too. This improves an existing case, not the full external-formula blueprint, arbitrary concurrent requests, remote workers, all function coercions or cross-browser/mobile editing. The 200-blueprint coverage ledger remains unchanged.

## Custom Event: cancellation, subscription and real pointer targets

`sheets/custom-event` now shares its runtime, controls, fixture and SDK CSS between Preview and export. Three fictional Aster field samples and a second Archive sheet replace the empty workbook. Frame callbacks, DOM handlers, event consumers, event sources and the owning Univer are released on disposal.

## PDF viewer lifecycle and owning-instance cleanup

`pdfs/create-load-viewer` implements the four-page supplier-audit fixture for `standalone-pdfs-001`: an original Kestrel cover, a native findings table, an embedded receiving-area schematic, and editable sign-off. The data is explicitly fictional. The image is authored inline SVG, not a remote dependency or a purported real inspection photograph. The reference is the installed SDK's PDF Facade declarations and `univer-pro/examples/src/pdfs-advanced` plugin registration; no upstream example is modified. The catalog entry has English/Chinese explanations, three variants, seven documented actions, four states, and a thin Preview using the same four source files as the independent project. All five SDK CSS imports are in the exported implementation.

Dispose saves the current snapshot in memory and destroys the owning **Univer**, not only `FUniver`. Remount recreates the editor in the same container and preserves the complete saved snapshot, including the decision, table and embedded image. Undo, selection and viewport are intentionally not retained. Reset and theme changes restore the seeded packet. The invalid variant enters the actual load boundary and rejects a missing document before disposing anything. It is not a general validator for arbitrary external JSON. A blank one-page document is distinct from an unmounted viewer; its absent sign-off action reports an error without changing content. Binary `.pdf` loading through Exchange and interrupted/external-load errors remain unfinished, so this capability is partial rather than fully verified.

`pnpm test:showcase:pdf-lifecycle` passes in `test-results/pdf-lifecycle-wrapped-detail/` and `test-results/pdf-lifecycle-wrapped-standalone/`, with no browser/console errors. Checks cover four native pages, real table/image presence, native image pixels after each of three complete teardown/remount cycles, absence of the disposed PDF runtime record and old canvas/workbench, exact snapshot preservation, invalid-load preservation, downloaded JSON, blank/reset, keyboard host controls, native page input, narrow widths and SDK CSS isolation. The development run also covers light/dark recreation and both localized detail pages with working decision updates in their actual iframes. Visual QA found clipped single-line body text in the first version; explicit line breaks preserve every word. The final check includes a real-canvas right-margin overflow probe, and all four pages plus the updated decision are reviewed visually. Earlier `pdf-lifecycle`, `pdf-lifecycle-active-page`, and pre-wrapping reports remain diagnostic history, not final visual evidence.

The independent project is `<USERPROFILE>/AppData/Local/Temp/univer-pdf-lifecycle-2W7Foq`, generated through the actual exporter with eight files including the Preview reference and a fresh 157-package installation. Vite 8.2.2 builds `index-0ZBSqe19.js` (11,928.48 kB / 2,612.27 kB gzip) and `index-D07EQ8eJ.css` (101.31 kB / 14.87 kB gzip), plus lazy language chunks. The bundle warning is unresolved. Only `pdfs/create-load-viewer` was compiled for this development run, with zero document MDX modules. This work uses SDK-native PDF snapshots and canvas rendering; it does not deliver a binary PDF artifact or claim Exchange import/export.

The strict `pnpm test:showcase:disposal` audit verifies the installed SDK ownership contract. The original 43 shared implementations and two additional Lit aliases now dispose the retained Univer. React adapters capture old controllers and defer native-root disposal outside the enclosing commit; selected async continuations stop after disposal. ## Product Launch template progress

Atlas contains eleven original fictional pages: product story, three personas, four pain points, five capabilities, review workflow, synthetic pilot measures with denominators, launch scope, six date-proportional milestones, readiness targets, next actions and closing. New starter deck replaces the current presentation with four pages: story prompts, evidence prompts without invented pilot results, a working example rollout and a genuinely empty canvas with authoring notes. The replacement behavior is disclosed before use; download the current snapshot to retain edits. The GA action works in both decks, updating label and marker through one native drawing batch while preserving other elements and notes. Reset and editor theme changes restore the full fixture. Local JSON downloads (`snapshot.json`, `launch-metrics.json`) reflect SDK state; metrics explicitly retain the original full-deck baseline alongside current milestones. These are not PPTX exchange exports. Capability coverage counts remain unchanged; this work does not establish completion of the entire Slides template backlog.

Missing media variant replaces the closing page with an authored placeholder, not an SDK network-error handler. A local PNG/JPEG replaces that native drawing and fits its fixed slot without distortion. The demo validates MIME, byte signature, decoding, non-empty size up to 5 MiB, and dimensions up to 4096 × 4096 pixels. Invalid files preserve the previous image. Image bytes are embedded in the SDK snapshot, with no upload or backend. Reload snapshot recreates the saved presentation and clears history; the old UI is allowed to unmount before creating its replacement. Each successful image replacement uses a separate native undo group: reorder the slot to the end, delete it, then insert the image at the original index. One Undo restores both the old element and its stacking order; Redo restores the image. The strictly failing SDK-only reproductions in `test:showcase:slides-media-history-sdk` (default and `delete-order` modes) document beta.2 cross-type Undo retaining image fields and single-element deletion Undo appending at the end; see `capabilities/sdk-issues.md`.
