Preview, displayed source and standalone use the same four authored files, including all five SDK stylesheets and local CSS. Following the background feedback, regular pages (story, proof, capabilities, rollout) use opaque white; colored theme pages remain intentional. The SDK's legacy Text adaptor hard-codes white fill/gray stroke, so authored text now uses native text-box shapes with explicit NoFill/NoLine and left/top alignment. This changes fixture drawing data, not editor CSS or SDK defaults. `applyTextToShapeText` preserves GA label typography/alignment when updating its native document content. The first borderless run found an automatically hyphenated proof footnote; an explicit authored line break fixes it without omitting any wording. The earlier failed report remains in `test-results/slides-launch-borderless/`.

Example controls now uses a native, keyboard-operable disclosure, collapsed by default. Its bounded scrolling panel contains the warnings, thirteen host form controls and optional JSON readback; the native Univer ribbon is separate and unchanged. Expanding/collapsing fits the canvas and changes saved viewport zoom, but preserves all other presentation data. In the 640px development preview, collapsing reduces content height from 849px to 640px and leaves 571px for the editor. Both English and Chinese detail iframes fit the collapsed editor without internal host scrolling. This is a local Product Launch improvement, not a new catalog-wide shell. The shared playground remains 640px high; expanding the controls can still require vertical scrolling.

## Catalog CSS export audit

`pnpm test:showcase:css` loads the actual registered file maps and runs the actual source exporter for all 81 cases, without loading or compiling Preview/SDK runtime modules. The check covers declared static CSS imports, exported local styles, resolvable SDK stylesheet paths and dependencies, Preview-only SDK imports, and hard-coded remote stylesheet links. The final `test-results/showcase-css/css-report.json` has no findings. It is a stylesheet/dependency check, not proof that all 81 cases' interactions or arbitrary dynamic assets work. CI runs this gate alongside source parity and compile-scope checks. The CSS audit uses its own filename so a reused results directory cannot overwrite native runtime/export evidence; `node scripts/test-showcase-css-report-isolation.mjs` checks this boundary.

The audit found SDK CSS missing from the exported implementations of Company Knowledge Base, Incident Postmortem, Corporate Annual Report, Services Agreement, Quarterly Business Review, Product Launch and Technical Architecture Overview. Their shared `create-demo.ts` now imports the same SDK styles as Preview; their standalone entries load the exported base stylesheet for full-height mounting. Docs Watermark's Preview imported the Sheets preset stylesheet; it now imports Docs CSS. Hide Headers already imported a valid preset stylesheet, but Preview used a separate plugin-mode initialization. Both entries now use one preset-based implementation, and the standalone entry imports its container stylesheet. These changes do not close those templates' separate feature/interaction acceptance work.

The two Lit examples no longer depend on unversioned unpkg CSS. Both retain Shadow DOM: ordinary SDK CSS imports support document-level portals, while `new URL(.../lib/index.css, import.meta.url)` bundles the installed SDK stylesheet for the shadow root. The Sheets example now actually uses the Sheets preset (the former exported code mistakenly selected Docs). Preview and standalone mount the same custom-element implementation and the same original empty fixture. Distinct product-specific custom-element names avoid registration collisions; disconnection disposes the editor, and reconnection initializes it again. These remain minimal Lit integration examples, not rich product templates or full Shadow DOM authoring/accessibility certification.

Next's normal CSS pipeline initially emitted a JavaScript proxy at the shadow stylesheet URL (HTTP 200 but zero CSS rules). A narrowly scoped webpack asset rule now handles only URL dependencies for these two preset stylesheets issued by these two Lit modules. ## Slides page size, scaling and overflow delivery

Run `pnpm dev:showcase slides/page-size-and-overflow` and `pnpm test:showcase:slides-size`. Eight individually authored pages use seven arrangements: title, section, content, comparison, data, quote and closing. Four variants cover widescreen, standard with unchanged content, boundary/rotation markers and zero pages. No external media are redistributed.

Native deck/default and active-page size commands demonstrate 16:9, 4:3, square, portrait and custom dimensions; page overrides survive changes to the deck default. Size-only commands leave owned geometry unchanged while an explicitly sized master rule resolves to the new page size. The separate Resize and scale page action computes uniform geometry/font scaling and centering, then executes a native page-size command and one native drawing batch. When both change, Undo twice restores the original. This is a controlled-fixture scaler for plain text and simple shapes, not a universal imported/rich-text presentation scaler. Unsupported edits are rejected before resizing; existing overflow is not silently clamped. Fit slide changes viewport zoom, not page dimensions or content geometry.

Diagnostics use the actual native render object's transformed corners, including a rotated square crossing the left edge. Exact edge contact is inside; negative, right and bottom overflow are listed explicitly. These are frame bounds, not glyph clipping, stroke/shadow extents or arbitrary nested-group coverage. Custom integer dimensions 120–2400 are a host input guard, not an SDK limit. Reload preserves the snapshot except its recreated root ID and clears history; Reset restores all inputs and the frozen clock. Native history also includes Show slide navigation.

## Slides layouts, masters and placeholder delivery

Run `pnpm dev:showcase slides/layouts-and-placeholders` and `pnpm test:showcase:slides-layouts`. Aster is an original fictional community-radio briefing: four programs, twenty volunteers (6/4/5/5), twelve pilot episodes (nine on schedule, three rescheduled) and eight distinct pages with individual notes. Seven authored layouts cover title, section, content, comparison, data, quote and closing. These are fixture layouts; Data, Quote and Closing are not additional SDK enum values. Four variants cover the complete varied-layout deck, the same eight pages in comparison layout, inherited placeholders without page-owned elements, and zero pages. The quote and people are fictional; no reference-site media is redistributed.

The SDK resolves real master, layout and slide layers. The master supplies a stripe and footer. Layouts contain a panel and title/body placeholder defaults; slide-owned placeholders suppress matching defaults by type/index despite having different element IDs. This fixture explicitly copies layout geometry into owned placeholders. Inspect reports stored data, resolved layers and actual render-object bounds separately. Native transform, rich-text and inherited-graphics commands support offset, restore placement, text changes, visibility and Undo/Redo. Text fixtures start with explicit rich-text payloads, and checks compare complete restored elements and actual canvas text.

**Rebuild with layout is host integration**, not a native Apply Layout command: it validates the page/layout/master and type/index matches before replacing the unit, preserves content/IDs/notes/other pages, copies target placements and clears history. The beta.2 facade has no declared existing-page layout-change/reset API; `CreateSlideByLayoutCommand` creates a new page with default layers instead. Restore layout placement writes matched geometry through the native transform API while retaining text/style. It does not clear all overrides or establish future automatic geometry inheritance. These boundaries keep `standalone-slides-004` **partial**.

## Slides snapshot round-trip delivery

Run `pnpm dev:showcase slides/save-restore-deck` and `pnpm test:showcase:slides-roundtrip`. Tern is an original fictional coastal-observation briefing, frozen at 2027-03-31 09:00 UTC. The case creates and edits a real SDK presentation, saves its complete snapshot in browser memory, restores it into a fresh unit and downloads the exact checkpoint JSON. This is not PPTX exchange, remote persistence or revision history. Reference validation occurs before disposing the live unit; invalid restore leaves both the live deck and checkpoint intact. SHA-256 excludes only the recreated root unit ID. Slide order, active page, elements, notes, resources and zoom are otherwise retained in the equality check. Reset restores fixture content and input defaults, clears the checkpoint/history and fits the slide. Theme changes also recreate the fixture. Restoring does not silently refit the saved zoom.

The same four authored source files drive Preview, Source and the independently generated project. Fitting changes snapshot zoom and may therefore change checkpoint equality; it is explicit after resizing. Dense slide text and trial watermarks still limit small-screen reading, and this is not a full mobile-authoring certification.

Both paths share source-path/folder metadata handling and the same guide tree transformations and icons. Willow is an original fictional nursery memo: three stations contain 84/126/53 seedlings (263 total), with two distinct shift plans, a supplies table and an original CC0 map. The native chart, table, columns and inline image reflow in the same unit. Host widths (fluid/960/600/390/320), reading widths (640/820/1040) and SDK zoom (50–200%) are separate controls. No-history layout mutations and `SetDocZoomRatioOperation` keep viewport changes out of text Undo history. Preview and standalone execute the same source factory.

`scripts/test-modern-responsive.mjs` checks 16 host-width/zoom combinations, three reading widths, actual scene scale, model/skeleton/rendered drawing sizes, native selection offsets and every highlight polygon. Selection reveal calls the native scroll controller for both endpoints, then uses the native viewport scroll API to remove excess from the controller's 100-document-unit buffer. Wrapped ranges that fit must be fully visible, while ranges taller than the viewport retain their full selection and expose the final line. This corrected the 200% selection clipping in both the shorter documentation preview and the standalone page without changing content, font sizes or selection ranges. Other exercised checks cover text Undo/Redo after layout changes, snapshot reload, invalid zoom, empty/reset, all 15 host controls in keyboard order with visible focus, keyboard zoom entry, documentation light/dark recreation and a single canvas after remount. This is not a complete SDK accessibility audit.

Strict acceptance remains **failing**. At 390px/150%, the SDK raises the previously isolated empty-divide `breakType` exception: model width becomes 249.33 but the skeleton remains 374; chart/map widths remain stale and selection goes outside the viewport. Widening/reducing zoom allows subsequent matrix cases to continue in the same unit. Separately, switching reading width to 640 changes chart model/skeleton width to 506.67 but leaves its visible frame at 520 after 30 read-only inspection retries. The native modern layout forces 66.67-unit margins on both sides, so very narrow hosts at high zoom have an impractically small text area; overriding ordinary document margins does not remove that SDK rule. No SDK source or installed package is patched. Both limitations are disclosed in the bilingual guide; the capability remains partial.

The independent project `<USERPROFILE>/AppData/Local/Temp/univer-modern-responsive-GM9OA1` was generated from the displayed files, installed with pnpm 12.1.0 (175 packages), and built with Vite 8.2.2. Its final entry is `index-CVqU6sAW.js`, about 14.39 MB / 3.36 MB gzip; the bundle-size concern remains. Current strict reports and screenshots are under `test-results/modern-responsive-scroll` and `test-results/modern-responsive-scroll-standalone`. Both finished with the same two remaining failure categories (390px/150% layout and 640 reading-width chart frame), and no uncaught browser/console errors. Earlier `modern-responsive-final*` and `modern-responsive-endpoints*` reports predate the full scroll correction; failed hot-update runs are not final source evidence. Browser-window resizing, fresh narrow entry and exhaustive native content-bound checks remain additional work, not implied passes from the embedded-host matrix.

## Modern document chart delivery checks

Run `pnpm dev:showcase docs-modern/charts-in-documents` to compile only the Saffron repair-hub case. Its original Q1 dataset contains 162 repairs and 73 workshop participants. Correcting February from 57 to 71 changes the totals to 176/73 and checksum to 249. A separate Q2 scenario includes a real zero during a planned bench closure. Seven chart types, one/two-measure mappings, title, legend, palette, dimensions, inline paragraph anchors, data updates, PNG export and document lifecycle controls share the same source factory. Independent content includes two volunteer plans, a three-task follow-up table and an original CC0 workshop schematic.

The implementation uses `FDocument.newChart()/insertChart()`, live Chart facade methods and `DocChartSnapshotRenderService.exportImage()` from the installed beta.2 plugins. Resize defaults to `UpdateDrawingDocTransformCommand`; the explicitly labeled Chart facade path remains available as a comparison. The inspection panel waits for the SDK's first non-null render configuration, then reports actual local data, totals, renderer specification, drawing object and document layout. Series changes can settle asynchronously; the regression waits for the requested renderer values rather than accepting stale readback. It does not substitute a host chart renderer or a screenshot for native export. Responsive host mutations change real page/table/chart layout without adding Undo history.

`pnpm test:showcase:modern-charts` strictly checks data and native renderer specifications against known values. The default drawing-size command correctly resizes the visible frame to 480x260, but its Undo restores model/skeleton dimensions to 560x300 while the frame remains 480x260. With `SHOWCASE_CHART_RESIZE_API=facade`, `FChart.setSize()` already fails the forward resize: model/skeleton become 480x260 but the frame stays 560x300. Both failures survive repeated readback, with renderer scale 1. `SHOWCASE_OBSERVE_KNOWN_DEFECTS=1` accepts only these recorded signatures while exercising the remaining controls; its report is not native-frame-history acceptance. No SDK code was patched or render object manually rewritten.

The independent source project is `<USERPROFILE>/AppData/Local/Temp/univer-modern-charts-iKo3s3`. It installed 175 packages and built with Vite 8.2.2; the entry remains approximately 14.40 MB / 3.36 MB gzip. Current reports and screenshots are under `test-results/modern-charts*`. A development hot-update run failed before mounting with `Invalid or unexpected token` and ChunkLoadError; after that test exited, only the scoped server was restarted. The development-server issue is not claimed fixed. Remaining capability acceptance is recorded in the coverage ledger.

Final observed-defect runs in `modern-charts-final` and `modern-charts-final-standalone` cover seven actual render types (including two stacked measures), series selection, legend orientation/visibility, data values and checksum, data Undo/Redo, palette/title, forward drawing-size changes, native PNG download, stable shifted anchors across reload, deletion/reinsertion, same-unit viewport widths 760/520/390/320, empty/reset, 30 keyboard-accessible controls, and reload. Documentation also exercises light/dark theme recreation with one canvas and zero browser/console errors. The PNG is 960x520 for a 480x260 chart; its two selected series colors are verified from pixels and its corrected title/bar values were visually inspected. The optional facade path is recorded separately in `modern-charts-final-facade-standalone`. Reports retain `passed-with-known-frame-gap`, not full acceptance; strict runs are retained under `modern-charts-final-strict*` and `modern-charts-final-facade-strict-standalone`.

Generated-source/manifest byte parity, scoped TypeScript, lint and registry/module-scope validation passed at chart delivery. The production entry is `index-CAufV-ke.js` (14,395.38 kB, 3,361.03 kB gzip), with the bundle-size warning still open. At that point the catalog had 73 registered cases and 21 assessed blueprints (4 verified, 17 partial); see the inventory above for current counts. Registration is not completion.

## Modern document shape delivery checks

Run `pnpm dev:showcase docs-modern/shapes-in-documents` to compile only the Aster museum-access case. It uses real `docs-shape` / `docs-shape-ui` models, not the OSS rectangle/ellipse commands that insert SVG images. Its original fictional fixture contains two editable shapes, a decision heading, two access-plan columns, a workstream table, an embedded CC0 route sketch and an independent visitor chart. Four geometries, three fill/stroke styles, editable labels, five wrapping modes, explicit size/rotation/position, four drawing-order actions, paragraph anchoring, content insertion before the anchor, snapshot reload, deletion/reinsertion, Undo/Redo, empty/error/reset and an SDK readback panel share one source factory.

The core Docs preset registers the ordinary formula engine. Shape UI requires Pro formula services, so a final small preset registers License first and replaces that formula plugin by name with `UniverProFormulaEnginePlugin`. Putting License again in the later plugins list caused duplicate registration; this integration error was corrected. `pnpm install --fix-lockfile` corrected a pre-existing root engine-formula link that resolved to a different local source checkout. No SDK source or submodule was changed.

`pnpm test:showcase:modern-shapes` strictly checks native shape canvas paint, separate shape/image types, live transforms and styles, real layer/order changes, anchor identity and shifted offsets, same-layer relative order after reload, resolved geometry handles, model data, history and independent references. Loading may materialize default adjustment values and renumber layer-local z-index; the gate compares resolved geometry and visible relative ordering, while also requiring the persisted drawing-order array to remain identical.

The strict gate currently fails **narrow responsive reflow**. The browser stack maps to `_getConsecutiveHyphenLineCount` in the installed beta.2 `@univerjs/engine-render` ES bundle (function starts at line 14576): it reads the last divide's `breakType` without checking whether `line.divides` is empty. The layout throws after the width model changes, retaining the prior rendered layout. Production measured model 382 against rendered 512; documentation measured model 380 against rendered 510. Fresh entry and removing both shapes also reproduce it; snapshot/history operations and shape anchors are not prerequisites. The fixture component that creates the empty line has not yet been minimized. Explicit skeleton invalidation/reRender did not fix it and was removed. No installed or upstream SDK source was patched.

`pnpm test:showcase:docs-empty-divide-sdk` extracts and executes that exact installed helper, checks ordinary lines, and strictly fails the empty-divide input. It isolates the helper exception, not full document layout. `SHOWCASE_OBSERVE_KNOWN_DEFECTS=1` asserts the specific exception rather than declaring it correct. The browser interaction gate's observed-defect mode likewise requires the original `breakType` error plus the stale-first-width signature. Its result is not responsive acceptance.

`pnpm test:showcase:modern-shape-layout-error` checks that repeated Inspect retains the layout error, the readback exposes both model and rendered geometry, and widening recovers the same unit and content, with and without native shapes. It also checks direct narrow entry, enabled recovery controls, Empty document and Reset. This is error-handling acceptance only. `pnpm test:showcase:modern-shape-accessibility` checks every host control's keyboard order, visible focus, keyboard label editing and SDK Undo, repeated documentation theme changes, baseline restoration and a single live canvas after recreation/reload. SDK React-root disposal is deferred past the parent effect cleanup. Theme changes restore the fixture; the independent entry intentionally defaults to light. These checks are not a full SDK screen-reader audit or a fix for narrow text layout. This capability remains partial.

The generated independent project is `<USERPROFILE>/AppData/Local/Temp/univer-modern-shapes-XfHf6a`, with a fresh 180-package install and successful Vite 8.2.2 production build. Regenerable browser reports/screenshots live in `test-results/modern-shapes*`. Earlier failed startup and development hot-update artifacts may coexist with later results; only the report for the specified final run is evidence. The shape/chart stack still has a large-bundle warning (about 15.17 MB, 3.58 MB gzip for the entry chunk); compiling only this route does not eliminate its production payload cost.

Earlier observed-gap reports are retained under `test-results/modern-shapes-final*`; they predate persistent error handling. Current error and accessibility reports use `test-results/modern-shape-layout-error*` and `test-results/modern-shape-accessibility*`; current interaction reports use `test-results/modern-shapes-persistent-error*`. Inspect the individual report status and failure artifacts before claiming a run passed. A build or an observed-defect run does not close the remaining coverage requirements.

On 2026-09-04, final error/recovery reports (`modern-shape-layout-error-final`, `modern-shape-layout-error-standalone`) and host keyboard/lifecycle reports (`modern-shape-accessibility-final`, `modern-shape-accessibility-standalone`) passed. Both current interaction runs passed only in observed-defect mode; both `modern-shapes-persistent-error-strict*` runs failed with the original SDK `breakType` error. The strict installed-helper test also failed, while its exact-defect observation passed. The 30 host controls, keyboard label edit/Undo, repeated documentation theme recreation and browser console errors are covered; standalone dark integration is deliberately not claimed. Exact generated-source/manifest parity, scoped TypeScript, lint, registry/scope validation and the independent production build passed. ## Modern document image delivery checks

Start `pnpm dev:showcase docs-modern/images-and-wrapping` to compile only this case. Kestrel is an original fictional coastal recording brief with an embedded CC0 recorder illustration, a long wrapping paragraph, two recording-method columns, a station table and an independent clips chart. The same source factory powers Preview and the downloadable project. It supports five wrapping styles, floating left/center/right alignment, validated dimensions, rotation, full/recorder/horizon crops, descriptive metadata, deletion/reinsertion, SDK Undo/Redo, empty/reset and snapshot reload. Crop offsets are pixels, not percentages.

`pnpm test:showcase:modern-images` is strict. It checks actual image paint, real text-line changes, image positions, document and render-image crop state, metadata/history, independent mixed-content preservation, invalid input, lifecycle and narrow layout. It currently fails because beta.2 scales crop offsets again while resizing: model left/right 60 becomes render left/right 30. `SHOWCASE_OBSERVE_KNOWN_DEFECTS=1` records this mismatch while checking other behavior; it is not complete image capability acceptance. The test also checks actual canvas image dimensions, waits for asynchronous image creation after snapshot reload, and compares its crop with persisted metadata. Alt text metadata is not a claim of canvas screen-reader support.

The default **Responsive reflow** mode now changes actual document width while keeping view scale at 1. Native `ColumnResponsiveType.STACK` uses 220-unit column minimums; the station table and chart adapt their widths to the text area. The host applies these layout-only changes through SDK document mutations with `noHistory: true`; resizing neither recreates the document nor adds/clears editing history. Text, business data, IDs and explicit image dimensions are preserved. Oversized images can therefore overflow a narrow text column by design. **Fixed-width zoom** restores the 820-unit document, 520-unit table and 560-unit chart, retaining original line breaks while the SDK scales the view. These are explicit host integration policies, not a claim that SDK documents automatically implement viewport reflow.

`pnpm test:showcase:modern-image-reflow` independently verifies widths 760/520/390/320, actual text-line changes, horizontal/stacked column geometry, rendered table/chart dimensions, unchanged business content and unit identity, Undo/Redo across resizing, snapshot reload, narrow empty/reset, fixed-zoom comparison and restoration to wide layout. Narrow controls and the explanatory hint scroll within bounded heights so at least 250 pixels of editor remain visible. The strict reflow gate does not disable or replace the existing strict crop gate. Regenerable reflow reports/screenshots are in `test-results/modern-image-reflow` and `test-results/modern-image-reflow-standalone`; current image regression reports use `test-results/modern-images-responsive` and `test-results/modern-images-responsive-standalone`, with the crop failure retained in `test-results/modern-images-responsive-strict`.

`Show chart` now targets the chart's real placeholder end instead of its preceding caption, so the full chart enters the narrow editor viewport. Final reflow and observed-defect image regression runs passed in documentation and independent production. An intermediate Next hot-update run failed before mount with `Invalid or unexpected token`; after that test exited, restarting only the scoped server restored passing runs. That development-server issue is not claimed fixed, and its earlier failure artifact may remain beside the latest passing report.

There is no beta.2 Facade setter for image description. The example registers a small host command that invokes the real SDK `RichTextEditingMutation`; this creates the command trigger required for SDK Undo/Redo. A direct mutation originally changed the data without creating history; that integration error was fixed rather than reported as an SDK defect. Cropping, wrapping and resizing continue to use SDK commands/Facades without patched packages or renderer compensation.

On 2026-09-04 the independent Kestrel source project passed a fresh pnpm 12.1.0 installation of 175 packages, byte-for-byte source comparison and Vite 8.2.2 production build. The responsive update was also generated into a new isolated project and freshly installed with the same dependencies; no dependency was added for reflow. Its main JavaScript chunk is approximately 14.40 MB (3.36 MB gzip); the chart-stack size warning remains. The earlier fixed-layout evidence is retained under `test-results/modern-images-strict`, `test-results/modern-images` and `test-results/modern-images-standalone`. Inspect reports for the actual status of each run; registry counts and static validation are not browser acceptance.

## Information architecture

The navigation has at most four levels:

1. Product
2. Features, Showcases, or Integrations
3. Capability group
4. Demo

A Demo represents one user-visible capability. Configuration choices and datasets are Variants; commands that change the result are Actions. CRUD verbs do not become separate pages unless they represent different user workflows or SDK models.

## Case contract

Every case exports the existing compatible contract:

```ts
export default {
  metadata,
  files,
  Preview,
}
```

`metadata` may add `product`, `category`, `group`, `guide`, `packages`, `apis`, `variants`, `actions`, and `states`. `files` contains copyable source. `Preview` is the component that actually runs in the page.

The Playground includes the real `preview/main.tsx` as `/reference/preview.tsx.txt`: it is a Next.js host adapter reference, not a standalone application module. Runnable source begins at `/src/index.ts`. Source is displayed without locale replacements; authored CSS is preserved and imported dependencies use installed package versions. New cases must put SDK initialization, controls, and behavior in `code/create-demo.ts`, then import that same function from both the standalone entry and the Preview adapter. Do not maintain two implementations of the demo. Older enhanced cases still need a complete control-parity audit.

## Targeted verification

Start a scoped server with only the routes being verified:

```sh
pnpm dev:showcase bases/filter-builder bases/multi-field-sort bases/view-field-layout
pnpm test:showcase:bases
```

Run the test command in a second terminal. The server uses port 3030 (`SHOWCASE_PORT` can override it). Set `SHOWCASE_BASE_URL` when testing another port. Stop the current server before changing the selected routes.

The scoped loader removes other registry imports before webpack constructs its dependency graph. The server and client compilation audits reject any unselected demo. Other demo URLs intentionally return 404 in this mode. This is development-only; a scoped production build is rejected. `pnpm test:showcase:scope` verifies the filter and module-audit behavior. Requesting one URL from an unrestricted dev server is not sufficient to establish compilation isolation.

For Traditional features, start `pnpm dev:showcase docs-traditional/page-setup docs-traditional/paragraph-typesetting docs-traditional/pagination-rules`, then run `pnpm test:showcase:traditional`. Both browser test commands accept individual case slugs. Screenshots/reports go under the ignored `test-results` directory. Do not run a full catalog build to check one demo.

The Relational Tables sort check currently fails on the beta.2 live-sort defect recorded in `capabilities/sdk-issues.md`. `pnpm test:showcase:bases-sort-sdk` is a strict, UI-free reproduction that also fails until the defect is fixed. Neither is a green acceptance gate at present; the filter/layout checks can be run separately with `pnpm test:showcase:bases filter-builder view-field-layout`.

For a focused Content Pipeline lifecycle regression, start `pnpm dev:showcase bases/content-pipeline` and run `node scripts/test-showcase-regressions.mjs bases/content-pipeline`. The regression script also accepts the other individual slugs listed in that script; keep its selection aligned with the scoped server.

For Canvases connectors, start `pnpm dev:showcase boards/connector-routing`, then run `pnpm test:showcase:boards`. This checks all twelve rendered endpoint pairs, routing variants, node movement through the API, native canvas drag, and a keyboard-only focus/move path, binding repair, styles, invalid input, Undo/Redo, empty/reset/remount, rapid live theme changes, narrow layout, and browser errors. It does not establish collision-free routing or complete screen-reader semantics for the SDK canvas.

For Canvases layout, start `pnpm dev:showcase boards/alignment-spacing`, then run `pnpm test:showcase:boards-alignment`. Eight publishing-workflow nodes (including five unequal-sized editorial cards) and twelve connectors replace the release-pipeline dataset. Checks compare actual render-object bounds against the model for all six alignments, equal edge gaps, custom-gap boundaries, two-command Undo, an atomic 32px grid-coordinate batch with one-step Undo/Redo, native soft/hard/breakaway snapping and Ctrl bypass at 100% zoom, keyboard focus/move, theme remount, narrow layout, errors, empty/reset/remount. Toggling the visual grid does not itself quantize geometry; the separately labeled `setElementsTransform()` action does.

For Canvases element organization, start `pnpm dev:showcase boards/group-lock-z-order`, then run `pnpm test:showcase:boards-groups`. The museum campaign uses eight differently sized assets, deliberate overlaps, two decisions, twelve routes, and one detached route. Checks cover all four exact z-order variants, generated group IDs, two-level parent/descendant readback, nested focus and keyboard movement, atomic metadata locking through the exported operation, blocked locked movement, group movement with two-step Undo/Redo, ungrouping, render/model bounds parity, errors, empty/reset/remount, theme, and narrow layout. The beta.2 Facade lock-helper defect is recorded in `capabilities/sdk-issues.md`; this case does not disguise it.

The grouping checks also verify that Undo/Redo refreshes group IDs and that grouping again reuses a restored container. IDs are derived from SDK membership, not retained in a separate host cache. An expanded regression discovered that nested disband Undo restores membership but changes sibling z-order. `pnpm test:showcase:boards-group-undo-sdk` is a strict UI-free reproduction and currently fails on beta.2. The browser check verifies membership recovery, not correct restored sibling order; this limitation is visible in the demo and the capability is now partial.

For Canvases discovery, start `pnpm dev:showcase boards/search-element-query`, then run `pnpm test:showcase:boards-query`. The original Orion release-review fixture has eight nodes, two decisions, twelve routes, and one detached route. The SDK's Find index returns three shapes for Risk, or five hits across four unique elements when a connector name and label both match. Checks cover case/whitespace/substring rules, shape/connector/all-type queries, deduplicated real SDK selection, exact union bounds, native keyboard movement, per-result focus, text editing with Undo/Redo, blank/unmatched queries, missing IDs, empty/reset/remount, renderer parity, theme, and narrow layout. Native edits require the explicitly labeled Refresh query action; this is not an automatically updating search panel. Host styles are scoped to the direct results section so they do not alter SDK layout.

For Modern Docs paragraphs and headings, start `pnpm dev:showcase docs-modern/paragraph-heading-blocks`, then run `pnpm test:showcase:modern-headings`. The Lumen brief has six sections, two audience list items, one consent task, a risk callout, two lines of TypeScript, a quote, and a hyperlink backed by the corresponding SDK plugins. Checks cover H1–H5/body, both marked sections, invalid promotion/demotion boundaries, rendered font sizes, paragraph alignment/indentation/line height, content preservation, host-outline parity, Undo/Redo, native selection and typing, missing markers, empty/reset, full snapshot round trip, theme, narrow layout, one canvas after remount, and browser errors. The host outline derives from SDK metadata; it is not a native outline plugin. Narrow screens use fit-width zoom, not responsive text reflow. Unlicensed runs retain the SDK trial watermark. Lifecycle precautions and the same-ID SDK defect are described in `capabilities/sdk-issues.md`.

For Modern Docs lists, start `pnpm dev:showcase docs-modern/lists-task-items` and run `pnpm test:showcase:modern-lists`. The original Mosaic museum opening brief has three object bullets, five installation steps, two partner steps, and three tasks (one completed), plus actual callout/code/quote/link structures. Checks cover two-item nesting, levels 0–8, marker glyphs and positions in the renderer, decimal/letter/Roman/diamond variants, item/level/list selection, restart/continue numbering, Step n: formatting, validation, task toggle, text preservation, Undo/Redo, snapshot reload, native typing, empty/reset, theme/narrow/remount, and browser errors. The exported SDK scroll controller reveals affected content. The script explicitly observes a known item-scope defect; `pnpm test:showcase:docs-list-scope-sdk` is the strict isolated gate and currently fails. Do not treat the browser pass as full capability acceptance.

`pnpm test:showcase:source` checks exact source preservation, CSS preservation, dependencies, entry paths, and twenty thin Preview adapters (ten Modern features, three Traditional features, three Relational Tables features, and four Canvases features). Generated standalone projects include Vite dev/build/preview scripts, a Node engine requirement, and a pnpm 12 workspace policy that permits only the known `protobufjs` transitive build script. Static source checks do not prove installation or browser behavior; independent-project checks supply that evidence per case, not for the whole catalog.

### Independent source-project verification

Copy the displayed runnable files, including `package.json` and `index.html`, to a separate directory outside the documentation workspace. Do not include the `/reference/preview.tsx.txt` host adapter as application code. Run `pnpm install`, `pnpm build`, and `pnpm preview --host 127.0.0.1 --port 4173 --strictPort` there. From the documentation repository, run:

```sh
SHOWCASE_DEMO_URL=http://127.0.0.1:4173 SHOWCASE_SOURCE_DIR=/absolute/path/to/project SHOWCASE_RESULTS_DIR=test-results/boards-connectors-standalone pnpm test:showcase:boards
```

In PowerShell, assign these variables using `$env:SHOWCASE_DEMO_URL`, `$env:SHOWCASE_SOURCE_DIR`, and `$env:SHOWCASE_RESULTS_DIR` before running the test command. The optional source-directory gate compares authored modules byte-for-byte and the generated manifest/HTML against the displayed files before testing the independent application.

For any copied case, set `SHOWCASE_SOURCE_DIR` to its independent project directory and `SHOWCASE_SOURCE_SLUG` to its route (for example `boards/alignment-spacing`), then run `pnpm test:showcase:source` to verify the complete copied project. To exercise the alignment project, point `SHOWCASE_DEMO_URL` at its production preview and run `pnpm test:showcase:boards-alignment`.

The alignment project also passed isolated installation, exact source comparison, a targeted Vite production build, and the same browser checks against that build on 2026-09-04. Its results and screenshots are under `test-results/boards-alignment` and `test-results/boards-alignment-standalone`. It shares the approximately 15 MB SDK bundle-size concern below; that catalog-level optimization concern does not invalidate its verified layout behavior.

## Fixture rules

- Use deterministic IDs, dates, and values. Do not generate demo data with runtime randomness.
- Recreated runtime units may have fresh sequential identities to isolate editing sessions; fixture content, block IDs, values, and dates remain deterministic. Reset restores content/configuration, not a discarded unit's identity or history.
- Give different cases different domains, schemas, record distributions, edge values, and visible outcomes.
- Add applicable normal, empty, boundary, and error states.
- Every Variant must visibly change data, layout, configuration, or state.
- Every Action must have an observable before/after result and Reset must restore the exact baseline.
- Fixtures and binary assets must record their origin and license. Competitor screenshots and templates are research references, not distributable assets.

## PDF text markup

### State selection and editor-first follow-up (2026-09-05)

The current implementation adds directly selectable default, empty, boundary and error fixtures. Default seeds a yellow renewal highlight, blue fee underline and red two-line strikeout; empty removes annotations but retains the two-page text; boundary keeps opacity 0 in the model and opacity 1 on the lines; error restores default before the SDK rejects 1.5. Repeated loads and JSON downloads compare complete cached snapshots. Unknown fixture IDs fail without replacing the current document. Add and Apply appearance are disabled when already satisfied, and target changes read the actual mark appearance. The four variants, seven action groups and four states each have separate English/Chinese explanations. Earlier six-action/unmarked-default checkpoints below are historical, not the current behavior.

Host controls use the same native disclosure pattern as existing demos. The default highlight is checked against the real clipped viewport, not only sampled from an offscreen canvas. SDK readiness follows `FUniver.getCurrentLifecycleStage()` through Rendered; the shared screenshot gate also rejects the startup skeleton. `pdf-markup-editor-first`, `pdf-markup-states-standalone`, `pdf-markup-states-details-final` and `pdf-markup-states-ownership` reports pass the corresponding real interactions, native pixels, exact snapshots, EN/ZH iframe actions and seven-owner teardown. `showcase-screenshots-XP0HiB` passes the native white/theme/flex/opacity gate; its inspected 1438×640 capture supplies the public thumbnail. The 14 synthetic screenshot cases pass under `screenshot-contract-HMByl3`. Synthetic fixtures do not prove full SDK accessibility or dark-mode rendering.

The current eight-file independent export is recorded in `test-results/pdf-markup-states-export/exports.json`, at `<USERPROFILE>/AppData/Local/Temp/univer-pdf-markup-states-9pqMzC`. A fresh 157-package install, strict standalone TypeScript and Vite 8.2.2 production build pass. `pdf-markup-states-export-ui/report.json` checks byte-exact exported source, five official stylesheets, a native `rgb(255, 255, 255)` workbench, `--univer-gray-0: #FFFFFF`, flex layout and full-size canvases. Main JS `index-B7BumWX5.js` is 11,929.55 kB / 2,612.35 kB gzip; CSS `index-7fmQmI8X.css` is 101.90 kB / 14.94 kB gzip. The large-bundle warning remains. Only this PDF case was compiled for these checks.

Detail verification exposed a separate shared frame-loading race: a ready child could remain behind an outer `opacity: 0`, 100px iframe after its initial resize message was missed. `PlaygroundFrame` now measures its same-origin child on load and after subscribing, and accepts resize messages only from its own child, with matching origin and a finite positive numeric height. `SHOWCASE_ORIGIN=http://localhost:3030 node scripts/test-playground-frame.mjs` tests the real frame using a deterministic child that emits no height message, plus valid and malformed/foreign messages; `test-results/playground-frame-final/report.json` passes. This tests frame integration, not an SDK imitation. The earlier `pdf-markup-states-details-cold` report retains both the React script warning and hidden-frame timeout. The intermediate hot-update run under `pdf-markup-states-details-frame` remains failed on script/state-update errors; the final stable EN/ZH run passes without suppressing errors. That does not establish a fix for all cold-compilation or HMR failures.

The latest unchanged-runtime rerun, `test-results/pdf-markup-states-described/report.json`, additionally asserts nonempty outcome descriptions for all 15 localized variant/action/state entries and a fully opaque, measured outer iframe. It passes both locales and all feature actions with no browser errors. `test-results/playground-frame-current/report.json` passes the final minimal frame-loading patch; its invalid-message checks assert unchanged height after each payload, not merely after a final valid resize.

### Initial implementation checkpoint

Run `pnpm dev:showcase pdfs/text-markup`, then `pnpm test:showcase:pdf-markup`. Set `SHOWCASE_DETAILS=1` to include English/Chinese feature explanations and actual iframe actions. The original Aster two-page draft has three distinct targets: a renewal date, a decimal annual fee and an obsolete clause spanning two lines. Four native annotation subtypes demonstrate highlight, underline, strikeout and squiggly; color, opacity 0/1, removal, per-item Undo/Redo, clearing, invalid-opacity rejection, snapshot reload and JSON download have observable effects. The frozen review date is stored in PDF metadata and read from the live snapshot. SDK-generated page IDs, creation timestamps and monotonic edit revisions are not a globally frozen clock.

These controls use installed public Facades for annotation mutations. The public PDF runtime service handles target-page navigation and reading-sized width fitting; it is not disguised as an automatic text-selection Facade. Native page scrolling remains available. The known PDF-point quadrilaterals demonstrate predefined line geometry, not arbitrary text selection. Source text remains unchanged, including under strikeout. Clear all issues independent removals; Undo restores one annotation at a time. Reset restores this session's complete unmarked original snapshot. Reload retains every snapshot field but clears history. Downloads contain SDK JSON rather than binary PDF; this case performs no upload, Exchange conversion or legal decision-making.

On 2026-09-05, `test-results/pdf-markup-current/report.json` passes the real documentation actions, native annotation-region pixel changes, color/opacity history, two-line geometry, invalid-value preservation, source-word integrity, exact reload/download/reset snapshots, 760/390/320 host layout and keyboard activation. Both localized guides expose four variants, six action groups and four states in a four-level tree; each iframe executes the real highlight action. `pdf-markup-current-standalone/report.json` passes the same feature suite in the independent production build. `pdf-markup-ownership/report.json` passes seven owning instances across theme/unmount cycles. Ownership does not yet test every pending-navigation or native-edit race. Removal Undo creates new audit metadata; only the edit-state revision and the restored annotation's creation timestamp/revision are excluded from that specific content equality assertion. Reload, download and Reset comparisons exclude no fields.

`test-results/pdf-markup-export/exports.json` points to the independently installed eight-file project at `<USERPROFILE>/AppData/Local/Temp/univer-pdf-markup-O5X63M` (157 packages). `pdf-markup-current-export-ui/report.json` verifies byte-for-byte source parity, all five official SDK stylesheet imports, native white workbench, theme/layout CSS and a full-size canvas. Vite 8.2.2 builds 1,780 modules: main `index-nBslT4Cx.js` is 11,927.36 kB / 2,611.57 kB gzip, and `index-dG8qNh7g.css` is 101.50 kB / 14.91 kB gzip, plus lazy language assets. The large-chunk warning remains. Scoped TypeScript, selected lint/formatting, source checks and the 84-case CSS/style-isolation gates pass. The actual module graph contains only `pdfs/text-markup` and zero document MDX modules. Native UI screenshots were visually inspected; a reviewed capture is available at `public/assets/showcase/pdfs-text-markup.png`.

Earlier failures are retained: `pdf-markup-native-pass` found a real narrow-fieldset overlap, corrected with scoped `flex-shrink: 0`; `pdf-markup-reading-size` failed on a development chunk syntax/load error before mount; `pdf-markup-details-cold` completed the action checks but failed the shared React script console gate. Restarting the scoped server and a warmed rerun are not proof that those development issues are fixed. ## PDF viewer lifecycle: meaningful history and editor-first layout

Start only `pnpm dev:showcase pdfs/create-load-viewer`, then run `pnpm test:showcase:pdf-lifecycle`. `SHOWCASE_DEMO_URL` can select an independent production preview. The original four-page Kestrel audit has different cover, findings-table, embedded schematic and sign-off content. The shared factory now disables absent/already-applied review targets, uses actual Undo/Redo Facades, and removes the duplicate Inspect button because SDK readback updates automatically. Blank loads restore the same cached snapshot. Startup/remount waits for Rendered, and asynchronous fitting cannot act on a replacement owner.

Every one of the three variants, eight action groups and four states has English/Chinese input/outcome explanations. Nine host buttons remain distinct from native SDK controls. The host control disclosure starts collapsed; its expanded contents scroll independently at narrow sizes. This follows existing showcase integration and leaves the native footer visible inside the 640px preview without changing SDK CSS. Readback, disposal and snapshot download are explicitly host integration around actual Facades/owner lifecycle, not extra native PDF capabilities.

`test-results/pdf-lifecycle-editor-first/report.json` and `pdf-lifecycle-editor-first-standalone/report.json` pass the current native interactions with zero browser errors: four different pages, image-byte/pixel preservation, right margins, Facade decision history, actual double-click/keyboard text edits, exact downloaded JSON, invalid-load preservation, three edited remounts, exact Reset and repeatable blank snapshots, disabled unavailable operations, host tab order, three narrow widths and visible native footer. The documentation run additionally verifies theme replacement and both localized detail pages, including non-empty descriptions and real embedded decision actions. A passing run here does not resolve the separately retained next-themes cold warning in other cases.

The independent nine-file export is `<USERPROFILE>/AppData/Local/Temp/univer-pdf-lifecycle-history-I2arCU`, recorded in `test-results/pdf-lifecycle-history-export/exports.json`. It has 157 separately installed packages. The final selected Vite 8.2.2 build transforms 1,780 modules: `index-D4a_nhWZ.js` is 11,929.49 kB / 2,612.72 kB gzip, and `index-BXkHesYY.css` is 101.56 kB / 14.90 kB gzip. `pdf-lifecycle-editor-first-ownership/report.json` passes seven owning instances across theme replacement and full unmount/remount, with old canvases detached and no browser errors. Static fixtures do not substitute for the real native UI checks above.

The inspected `pdf-lifecycle-editor-first-capture/showcase-screenshots-Gf0Y4l` native screenshot passes white workbench/theme/layout and ancestor-opacity checks, with a visible footer and no browser errors. It supplies `public/assets/showcase/pdfs-create-load-viewer.png`. Earlier expanded-control screenshots are not the final layout. This is an update to an existing assessment, not a newly covered capability: the full ledger remains 0 verified, 46 partial and 159 unassessed out of 205, with 84 registered cases.

The final independent production run `financial-report-standalone-current/report.json` passes all seven extended groups with zero browser/console errors, including fourteen native page captures and both native text edits before reload/download. `financial-report-export-ui-current/report.json` again verifies exact exported source and actual SDK styling. This establishes the current standalone case, not a waiver of the documentation cold-start failure or the larger fixture plan below.

Blueprint `standalone-pdfs-022` remains partial. In particular, the **larger** first-wave package (40 pages, three years, five segments, four regions, twenty disclosures, twelve annotations, page-18 review/comments, binary PDF and separate annotation/outline files) remains required. The fourteen-page case does not replace it; neither the opacity error nor the blank-page fixture substitutes for the first-wave invalid-annotation/no-document states. Current coverage is 84 registered demos, 205 blueprints, **0 verified / 46 partial / 159 unassessed**.

## Definition of done

A Demo is complete only when it:

- opens at a stable direct URL with the feature already visible;
- registers only the packages and plugins required by the case;
- provides a focused objective, steps, expected result, packages, and related APIs;
- displays the exact source used by the live Preview;
- supports initialize, interact, reset, dispose, and remount;
- has deterministic data distinct from unrelated cases;
- passes schema, source parity, fixture, interaction, reset, console, and targeted build checks.

`capabilities/blueprints.json` is the coverage backlog. A catalog entry is not considered implemented merely because it exists in the backlog; it must have a registered, runnable case satisfying this definition of done.
